00001	"""The Windows side broker of the parent owned native custody.
00002	
00003	The broker is the only creator of the native services of one round.  The
00004	parent starts it, keeps its original handle and talks to it over a private
00005	channel; the broker creates the services itself, holds their original
00006	handles and stops them again.
00007	
00008	Four properties of the broker are the reason it exists at all.
00009	
00010	* **Suspend first, adopt second.**  Every service is created with
00011	  ``CREATE_SUSPENDED``.  The broker holds the original process and thread
00012	  handle, it assigns the process to the job object of the round and only
00013	  then it resumes the thread.  A service is never started first and
00014	  adopted afterwards: a process that already ran could have broken away
00015	  from the job before the adoption.
00016	
00017	* **The job belongs to the round.**  One job object is created per round
00018	  with ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE`` and without
00019	  ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK``.  The children of the services inherit
00020	  that constraint.  When the broker closes the job handle, the round is
00021	  gone.  The broker is the only owner of that handle and of the stop.
00022	
00023	* **Nothing else is inherited.**  The services get their standard output
00024	  and their standard error and the null device as their standard input.
00025	  They never get the control channel of the broker, the job handle or any
00026	  other handle of the broker.  On Windows the inheritance set is not
00027	  trusted to the default: it is passed explicitly as the handle list of
00028	  the process attributes, and every handle of that list is created as
00029	  inheritable while every other handle is not.
00030	
00031	* **Only what was created is stopped.**  The broker has no notion of a
00032	  process name, of a pid or of a record of another owner.  It stops the
00033	  handles that it holds and the job that it created, and nothing else.
00034	
00035	Nothing in this module has ever run on Windows.  Every native claim of
00036	this package therefore stays ``NOT_PROVEN``: the Windows path is written
00037	from the documented behaviour of the API and it is not a measurement.
00038	"""
00039	import collections
00040	import hashlib
00041	import json
00042	import math
00043	import os
00044	import queue
00045	import re
00046	import select
00047	import subprocess
00048	import sys
00049	import threading
00050	import time
00051	from pathlib import Path
00052	
00053	PACK_ROOT = Path(__file__).resolve().parents[1]
00054	if str(PACK_ROOT) not in sys.path:
00055	    sys.path.insert(0, str(PACK_ROOT))
00056	
00057	from tools.v0_s3_native_custody import (  # noqa: E402 - the path is set above
00058	    CHANNEL_SCHEMA, CustodyError, MAX_DESCENDANTS, MAX_FRAME_BYTES, MAX_FRAMES,
00059	    MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES, MAX_STREAM_BYTES, decode_command, validate_launch_plan)
00060	
00061	# The bounded limits of the broker.  They are internal limits of this
00062	# foundation; they are not a claim about any native Windows behaviour.
00063	WAIT_MILLISECONDS = 5000
00064	HEARTBEAT_TIMEOUT_SECONDS = 120
00065	CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS = 5.0
00066	READER_JOIN_SECONDS = 2.0
00067	# The ONE cleanup budget of a round.  The stop and the cleanup of a round
00068	# share ONE absolute deadline: the deadline is generated once, at the
00069	# first stop or at the first failure of the cleanup, and it is the same
00070	# value in every layer of the cleanup.  No layer adds a budget of its own
00071	# and no layer starts a new wait with a fresh timeout.
00072	CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS = 5.0
00073	# The number of the startup results that the in-flight owner of a startup
00074	# step may carry.  The channel is bounded: a startup owner that produced
00075	# more than one result at a time would be an unbounded queue.
00076	STARTUP_INFLIGHT_SLOTS = 1
00077	# The bounded wait for a startup step that did not end before the
00078	# deadline of the round.  A step that is still running after this wait
00079	# keeps its owner and the round stays unknown: the round never claims a
00080	# cleanup that it cannot prove.
00081	STARTUP_SETTLE_SECONDS = 1.0
00082	MAX_PLAN_BYTES = 64 * 1024
00083	MAX_RECEIPT_BYTES = 1024 * 1024
00084	POLL_SECONDS = 0.25
00085	
00086	# The bounded waits of the private channel.  The pipe is polled in slices
00087	# of at most 50 milliseconds, so that the cancel flag and the absolute
00088	# deadline of the round are observed even while a peer is silent.
00089	POLL_SLICE_SECONDS = 0.05
00090	PIPE_READ_BYTES = 65536
00091	# The number of the frames of the channel that is reserved for the
00092	# terminal exchange of the round.  The heartbeat may not spend them: a
00093	# round that reached its frame budget has to fail closed and it may not
00094	# reconnect and it may not renew its lease.
00095	RESERVED_TERMINAL_FRAMES = 8
00096	
00097	# The frozen environment of every child.  The variables that the existing
00098	# authorised launch wrappers remove are removed here as well, so that a
00099	# child never inherits a proxy, a user site directory or a start-up file.
00100	REMOVED_ENVIRONMENT = ('PYTHONPATH', 'PYTHONHOME', 'PYTHONSTARTUP', 'PYTHONINSPECT',
00101	                        'PYTHONUSERBASE', 'PYTHONOPTIMIZE', 'HTTP_PROXY', 'HTTPS_PROXY',
00102	                        'ALL_PROXY')
00103	
00104	#: The one lock that guards the creation of the lock of the admission of
00105	#: an endpoint.  It is a lock of the **module** and it is never the lock
00106	#: of an endpoint: two endpoints never share an admission.
00107	_ADMISSION_LOCK_INIT = threading.Lock()
00108

00110	class BrokerError(RuntimeError):
00111	    """A refused broker step with a stable, machine readable code."""
00112	
00113	    def __init__(self, code, detail=None):
00114	        super().__init__(code)
00115	        self.code = code
00116	        self.detail = detail

00118	
00119	# -- the one context of a round ---------------------------------------------
00120	
00121	class _RoundContext:
00122	    """The ONE finite startup and cleanup context of one round.
00123	
00124	    The context holds the four things that every layer of a round has to
00125	    share and that may never be copied from one layer into the next one.
00126	
00127	    * The **clock** of the round.  Every deadline of the round is
00128	      expressed in it, so that a deadline of one domain is never
00129	      compared with a clock of another one.
00130	
00131	    * The **absolute deadline** of the round.  It is bound once and it
00132	      may only be shortened.
00133	
00134	    * The **one cleanup deadline**.  It is published **once**, by the
00135	      first caller that enters the cleanup, and every later caller
00136	      reuses that very value: two callers that entered the cleanup at the
00137	      same moment always see the same number, and the publication is one
00138	      atomic step under the lock of the context.
00139	
00140	    * The three **one way latches** of the round: the cancel, the stop
00141	      and the unknown.  A latch that was set is never cleared, and a
00142	      late event may never overwrite the unknown of the round.
00143	
00144	    Every layer of the round holds this **one object**: the core, the
00145	    loop, the transport of the channel and the endpoints of the channel.
00146	    A layer that only copied the number of a deadline would be a layer
00147	    with a deadline of its own, and two layers with two deadlines are
00148	    two budgets.
00149	    """
00150	
00151	    def __init__(self, *, clock=None, wall=None):
00152	        self.clock = clock or time.monotonic
00153	        self.wall = wall or time.time
00154	        self._lock = threading.RLock()
00155	        self._absolute_deadline = None
00156	        self._cleanup_deadline = None
00157	        self._inflight = []
00158	        self._inflight_lock = threading.Lock()
00159	        # The one way latches of the round.
00160	        self.cancel = threading.Event()
00161	        self.stopping = threading.Event()
00162	        self.unknown = threading.Event()
00163	
00164	    # -- the deadlines ----------------------------------------------------
00165	    def bind_absolute_deadline(self, deadline):
00166	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round.  It may only shorten."""
00167	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
00168	        if value is None:
00169	            return self._absolute_deadline
00170	        with self._lock:
00171	            if self._absolute_deadline is None or value < self._absolute_deadline:
00172	                self._absolute_deadline = value
00173	            return self._absolute_deadline
00174	
00175	    @property
00176	    def absolute_deadline(self):
00177	        with self._lock:
00178	            return self._absolute_deadline
00179	
00180	    def cleanup_deadline_value(self):
00181	        """The published cleanup deadline, or ``None`` before the first."""
00182	        with self._lock:
00183	            return self._cleanup_deadline
00184	
00185	    def cleanup_deadline(self, deadline=None):
00186	        """Publish the ONE cleanup deadline of the round, atomically.
00187	
00188	        The deadline is the earlier of the absolute deadline of the
00189	        round, the fixed cleanup budget of the round and the deadline
00190	        that the caller brought.  It is generated **once**: every later
00191	        caller receives the very same value and may never extend it and
00192	        may never replace it.
00193	        """
00194	        with self._lock:
00195	            if self._cleanup_deadline is None:
00196	                value = self.clock() + CLEANUP_BUDGET_SECONDS
00197	                if self._absolute_deadline is not None:
00198	                    value = min(value, self._absolute_deadline)
00199	                candidate = _require_deadline(deadline)
00200	                if candidate is not None:
00201	                    value = min(value, candidate)
00202	                self._cleanup_deadline = value
00203	            return self._cleanup_deadline
00204	
00205	    def remaining_cleanup(self, deadline=None):
00206	        """The seconds that are left until the cleanup deadline.
00207	
00208	        The value is recomputed on every call: a wait that already
00209	        consumed part of the budget never receives the whole budget
00210	        again.
00211	        """
00212	        value = self.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
00213	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
00214	
00215	    def cleanup_expired(self):
00216	        """Whether the shared cleanup deadline of the round arrived."""
00217	        with self._lock:
00218	            value = self._cleanup_deadline
00219	        if value is None:
00220	            return False
00221	        return self.clock() >= value
00222	
00223	    def remaining_absolute(self):
00224	        """The seconds that are left until the absolute deadline.
00225	
00226	        The value is recomputed on every call and it is read from the
00227	        **one** context of the round: a layer that copied the number of
00228	        the deadline would have a deadline of its own.  ``None`` means
00229	        that the round did not bind an absolute deadline at all.
00230	        """
00231	        with self._lock:
00232	            value = self._absolute_deadline
00233	        if value is None:
00234	            return None
00235	        return max(0.0, value - self.clock())
00236	
00237	    def effective_deadline(self, call=None, bound=None):
00238	        """The ONE deadline that a call of a layer really uses.
00239	
00240	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
00241	        layer, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
00242	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
00243	        the four.
00244	
00245	        The published cleanup deadline is part of it.  A round that
00246	        entered its cleanup publishes one deadline, and every layer that
00247	        reads the context afterwards is bound by it: a transport that
00248	        still waited with the long lease of the round would outlive the
00249	        only cleanup budget of the round.
00250	
00251	        The value is **recomputed on every call**: a deadline that was
00252	        published after the call started still shortens the call that is
00253	        running.
00254	        """
00255	        value = _require_deadline(call)
00256	        for candidate in (bound, self.absolute_deadline,
00257	                          self.cleanup_deadline_value()):
00258	            if candidate is None:
00259	                continue
00260	            if value is None or candidate < value:
00261	                value = candidate
00262	        return value
00263	
00264	    # -- the one way latches ----------------------------------------------
00265	    def request_cancel(self):
00266	        """Ask for the cancellation of the round.  One way."""
00267	        self.cancel.set()
00268	
00269	    def request_stop(self):
00270	        """Ask for the stop of the round.  One way."""
00271	        self.stopping.set()
00272	
00273	    def latch_unknown(self):
00274	        """Latch the unknown state of the round.  One way.
00275	
00276	        The first caller learns that it latched the state and every later
00277	        caller learns that the state was already latched.  A late event
00278	        may never clear the latch and may never replace it.
00279	        """
00280	        if self.unknown.is_set():
00281	            return False
00282	        self.unknown.set()
00283	        return True
00284	
00285	    def is_unknown(self):
00286	        """Whether the unknown state of the round was latched."""
00287	        return self.unknown.is_set()
00288	
00289	    # -- the owners of the operations in flight ---------------------------
00290	    def register_inflight(self, owner):
00291	        """Take the ownership of one operation that is in flight."""
00292	        with self._inflight_lock:
00293	            if owner not in self._inflight:
00294	                self._inflight.append(owner)
00295	        return owner
00296	
00297	    def release_inflight(self, owner):
00298	        """Give the ownership of one operation back."""
00299	        with self._inflight_lock:
00300	            if owner in self._inflight:
00301	                self._inflight.remove(owner)
00302	
00303	    def inflight(self):
00304	        """The owners of the operations that are in flight."""
00305	        with self._inflight_lock:
00306	            return list(self._inflight)
00307

00309	# -- the Windows primitive table -------------------------------------------
00310	
00311	def _windows_command_line(executable, arguments):
00312	    """Build the command line of one child.
00313	
00314	    The quoting is the quoting of the Microsoft C runtime: a backslash run
00315	    in front of a quote is doubled and a trailing backslash run is doubled
00316	    as well, so that a path with a backslash is never truncated by the
00317	    parser of the child.
00318	    """
00319	    parts = [_quote_windows_argument(executable)]
00320	    parts.extend(_quote_windows_argument(item) for item in arguments)
00321	    return ' '.join(parts)
00322	
00323	
00324	def _quote_windows_argument(value):
00325	    text = str(value)
00326	    if text and not any(character in text for character in ' \t"'):
00327	        return text
00328	    quoted = ['"']
00329	    backslashes = 0
00330	    for character in text:
00331	        if character == '\\':
00332	            backslashes += 1
00333	            continue
00334	        if character == '"':
00335	            quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2 + 1))
00336	            quoted.append('"')
00337	            backslashes = 0
00338	            continue
00339	        if backslashes:
00340	            quoted.append('\\' * backslashes)
00341	            backslashes = 0
00342	        quoted.append(character)
00343	    quoted.append('\\' * (backslashes * 2))
00344	    quoted.append('"')
00345	    return ''.join(quoted)
00346	
00347	
00348	def _environment_block(environment):
00349	    """Build the Unicode environment block of one child."""
00350	    if environment is None:
00351	        return None
00352	    import ctypes
00353	    items = sorted(environment.items(), key=lambda item: item[0].casefold())
00354	    raw = ''.join('%s=%s\0' % (key, value) for key, value in items) + '\0'
00355	    return ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(raw)
00356

00358	def _windows_api_types():
00359	    """The pure declaration of the Windows structures of the broker.
00360	
00361	    The declaration is built **without** a native library.  It declares
00362	    the layout of the structures and it returns them together with the
00363	    ``ctypes`` module that built them, so that the layout can be measured
00364	    and checked off Windows and so that the loaded primitive table uses
00365	    exactly this declaration.
00366	
00367	    The fixed width types are declared **explicitly**.  ``ctypes.wintypes``
00368	    is a module of the host: its ``BOOL`` is a ``c_long`` and its
00369	    ``DWORD`` is a ``c_uint``, and the size of a ``c_long`` is not the
00370	    size of a Windows ``BOOL`` on every host.  A layout that was built
00371	    from the types of the host would therefore be a layout of the host
00372	    and not the layout of Windows.  Here a ``DWORD`` and a ``BOOL`` are
00373	    always 32 bit, a ``WORD`` is always 16 bit and a handle and a
00374	    ``SIZE_T`` have the width of a pointer.
00375	
00376	    A name of a class body is resolved in the class namespace and in the
00377	    global namespace only.  A class body therefore never assigns a name
00378	    that shadows an enclosing local: ``kernel32 = kernel32`` would raise
00379	    a ``NameError`` instead of copying the value, which is why the table
00380	    of the broker is built from an explicit namespace and never from the
00381	    body of a class.
00382	    """
00383	    import ctypes
00384	
00385	    DWORD = ctypes.c_uint32
00386	    BOOL = ctypes.c_int32
00387	    WORD = ctypes.c_uint16
00388	    UINT = ctypes.c_uint32
00389	    LONG = ctypes.c_int32
00390	    ULONG = ctypes.c_uint32
00391	    HANDLE = ctypes.c_void_p
00392	    SIZE_T = ctypes.c_size_t
00393	    ULONG_PTR = ctypes.c_size_t
00394	    LPCWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
00395	    LPWSTR = ctypes.c_wchar_p
00396	
00397	    class STARTUPINFOW(ctypes.Structure):
00398	        _fields_ = [
00399	            ('cb', DWORD),
00400	            ('lpReserved', LPWSTR),
00401	            ('lpDesktop', LPWSTR),
00402	            ('lpTitle', LPWSTR),
00403	            ('dwX', DWORD),
00404	            ('dwY', DWORD),
00405	            ('dwXSize', DWORD),
00406	            ('dwYSize', DWORD),
00407	            ('dwXCountChars', DWORD),
00408	            ('dwYCountChars', DWORD),
00409	            ('dwFillAttribute', DWORD),
00410	            ('dwFlags', DWORD),
00411	            ('wShowWindow', WORD),
00412	            ('cbReserved2', WORD),
00413	            ('lpReserved2', ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_byte)),
00414	            ('hStdInput', HANDLE),
00415	            ('hStdOutput', HANDLE),
00416	            ('hStdError', HANDLE),
00417	        ]
00418	
00419	    class STARTUPINFOEXW(ctypes.Structure):
00420	        _fields_ = [('StartupInfo', STARTUPINFOW),
00421	                    ('lpAttributeList', ctypes.c_void_p)]
00422	
00423	    class PROCESS_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
00424	        _fields_ = [('hProcess', HANDLE),
00425	                    ('hThread', HANDLE),
00426	                    ('dwProcessId', DWORD),
00427	                    ('dwThreadId', DWORD)]
00428	
00429	    class SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES(ctypes.Structure):
00430	        """The security attributes of one inheritable handle.
00431	
00432	        ``bInheritHandle`` is a ``BOOL`` and it is a 32 bit value: a
00433	        handle that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes can
00434	        never be inherited at all.
00435	        """
00436	        _fields_ = [('nLength', DWORD),
00437	                    ('lpSecurityDescriptor', ctypes.c_void_p),
00438	                    ('bInheritHandle', BOOL)]
00439	
00440	    class FILETIME(ctypes.Structure):
00441	        _fields_ = [('dwLowDateTime', DWORD),
00442	                    ('dwHighDateTime', DWORD)]
00443	
00444	    class OVERLAPPED(ctypes.Structure):
00445	        """The overlapped structure of one asynchronous operation.
00446	
00447	        The structure owns the event of the completion and the offset
00448	        of the operation.  It is released only after the completion of
00449	        the operation was proven.
00450	        """
00451	        _fields_ = [('Internal', SIZE_T),
00452	                    ('InternalHigh', SIZE_T),
00453	                    ('Offset', DWORD),
00454	                    ('OffsetHigh', DWORD),
00455	                    ('hEvent', HANDLE)]
00456	
00457	    class IO_COUNTERS(ctypes.Structure):
00458	        _fields_ = [('ReadOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
00459	                    ('WriteOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
00460	                    ('OtherOperationCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
00461	                    ('ReadTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
00462	                    ('WriteTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong),
00463	                    ('OtherTransferCount', ctypes.c_ulonglong)]
00464	
00465	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
00466	        _fields_ = [('PerProcessUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
00467	                    ('PerJobUserTimeLimit', ctypes.c_longlong),
00468	                    ('LimitFlags', DWORD),
00469	                    ('MinimumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
00470	                    ('MaximumWorkingSetSize', SIZE_T),
00471	                    ('ActiveProcessLimit', DWORD),
00472	                    ('Affinity', SIZE_T),
00473	                    ('PriorityClass', DWORD),
00474	                    ('SchedulingClass', DWORD)]
00475	
00476	    class JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION(ctypes.Structure):
00477	        _fields_ = [('BasicLimitInformation', JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION),
00478	                    ('IoInfo', IO_COUNTERS),
00479	                    ('ProcessMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
00480	                    ('JobMemoryLimit', SIZE_T),
00481	                    ('PeakProcessMemoryUsed', SIZE_T),
00482	                    ('PeakJobMemoryUsed', SIZE_T)]
00483	
00484	    class JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST(ctypes.Structure):
00485	        """The process id list of a job object.
00486	
00487	        The list is an **inline** array of ``ULONG_PTR`` and not a
00488	        pointer: the first process id is written at the offset of the
00489	        array and every other id follows it.  A structure that declared
00490	        a pointer field here would read the first id as an address and
00491	        would dereference it.
00492	        """
00493	        _fields_ = [('NumberOfAssignedProcesses', DWORD),
00494	                    ('NumberOfProcessIdsInList', DWORD),
00495	                    ('ProcessIdList', ULONG_PTR * 1)]
00496	
00497	    return {
00498	        'ctypes': ctypes,
00499	        'DWORD': DWORD,
00500	        'BOOL': BOOL,
00501	        'WORD': WORD,
00502	        'UINT': UINT,
00503	        'LONG': LONG,
00504	        'ULONG': ULONG,
00505	        'HANDLE': HANDLE,
00506	        'SIZE_T': SIZE_T,
00507	        'ULONG_PTR': ULONG_PTR,
00508	        'LPCWSTR': LPCWSTR,
00509	        'LPWSTR': LPWSTR,
00510	        'STARTUPINFOW': STARTUPINFOW,
00511	        'STARTUPINFOEXW': STARTUPINFOEXW,
00512	        'PROCESS_INFORMATION': PROCESS_INFORMATION,
00513	        'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES': SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES,
00514	        'FILETIME': FILETIME,
00515	        'OVERLAPPED': OVERLAPPED,
00516	        'IO_COUNTERS': IO_COUNTERS,
00517	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
00518	        'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION': JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
00519	        'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST': JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST,
00520	    }

00522	
00523	def _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types):
00524	    """Declare the prototypes of the Windows primitives of the broker.
00525	
00526	    Every prototype is explicit.  A handle is a ``HANDLE``, a pointer is
00527	    an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer, a size is a ``SIZE_T`` and every
00528	    value that Windows defines as a 32 bit number is a ``DWORD`` or a
00529	    ``BOOL``.  A primitive without a declared prototype takes its
00530	    arguments as ``c_int`` and would truncate a 64 bit handle.
00531	
00532	    The declaration is applied to the primitive table that is handed in,
00533	    so the very same code declares the table of a real run and the table
00534	    of an offline inspection.
00535	    """
00536	    ctypes = types['ctypes']
00537	    void_p = ctypes.c_void_p
00538	    size_t = types['SIZE_T']
00539	    handle = types['HANDLE']
00540	    boolean = types['BOOL']
00541	    dword = types['DWORD']
00542	    uint = types['UINT']
00543	    ulong = types['ULONG']
00544	
00545	    kernel32.CreateJobObjectW.argtypes = [ctypes.POINTER(types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']),
00546	                                          types['LPCWSTR']]
00547	    kernel32.CreateJobObjectW.restype = handle
00548	    kernel32.SetInformationJobObject.argtypes = [handle, ctypes.c_int, void_p, dword]
00549	    kernel32.SetInformationJobObject.restype = boolean

00617	class _WindowsApiTable:
00618	    """The namespace of the primitive table of the broker.
00619	
00620	    The attributes are assigned explicitly.  A class body resolves a
00621	    name that it assigns itself in the class namespace and in the global
00622	    namespace only, so a class body that copies an enclosing local into
00623	    a name of its own raises a ``NameError`` instead of copying the
00624	    value.
00625	    """
00626	
00627	    def __init__(self, *, kernel32, types):
00628	        self.kernel32 = kernel32
00629	        self.types = dict(types)
00630	        for name in ('STARTUPINFOW', 'STARTUPINFOEXW', 'PROCESS_INFORMATION',
00631	                     'SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES', 'IO_COUNTERS',
00632	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
00633	                     'JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION',
00634	                     'JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST'):
00635	            setattr(self, name, types[name])
00636	
00637	    def __repr__(self):  # pragma: no cover - a diagnostic only
00638	        return '<WindowsApiTable types=%d>' % len(self.types)
00639	
00640	
00641	def _load_windows_api():
00642	    """Load the Windows primitive table of the broker.
00643	
00644	    The load is lazy and it happens only inside the explicit native entry.
00645	    An import of this module on a platform that is not Windows never
00646	    touches ``WinDLL`` and never resolves a symbol, so the module stays
00647	    importable and testable everywhere.  An unsupported platform refuses
00648	    here, before anything is created: it is never degraded into an
00649	    unconstrained process.
00650	
00651	    The declaration of the structures and the declaration of the
00652	    prototypes are two separate functions, so that the layout and the
00653	    prototypes can be inspected without loading a native library at all.
00654	    """
00655	    if os.name != 'nt':
00656	        raise BrokerError('BROKER_PLATFORM_UNSUPPORTED')
00657	    import ctypes
00658	    kernel32 = ctypes.WinDLL('kernel32', use_last_error=True)
00659	    types = _windows_api_types()
00660	    _declare_windows_primitives(kernel32, types)
00661	    return _WindowsApiTable(kernel32=kernel32, types=types)
00662	
00663	
00664	class CreatedProcess:
00665	    """The original handles of one created process."""
00666	
00667	    __slots__ = ('process', 'thread', 'pid', 'creation_time', 'failures')
00668	
00669	    def __init__(self, process, thread, pid, creation_time, failures=()):
00670	        self.process = process
00671	        self.thread = thread
00672	        self.pid = pid
00673	        self.creation_time = creation_time
00674	        self.failures = tuple(failures)
00675	
00676	
00677	class WindowsApi:
00678	    """The typed Windows primitive façade of the broker.
00679	
00680	    Every method of this class wraps exactly one Windows API call, and the
00681	    broker never calls a Windows function itself.  That keeps the native
00682	    detail - the pointer sizes, the handle ownership and the last error -
00683	    in one place, and it lets the offline validation drive the very same
00684	    broker logic through an in-process façade of the same shape.
00685	
00686	    The declarations are pointer size safe: a handle is a
00687	    ``wintypes.HANDLE``, every pointer is an explicit ``ctypes`` pointer
00688	    and every structure declares its fields.  The last error of a failed
00689	    call is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is only correct
00690	    because the module was loaded with ``use_last_error=True``.
00691	    """
00692	
00693	    CREATE_SUSPENDED = 0x00000004
00694	    EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT = 0x00080000
00695	    STARTF_USESTDHANDLES = 0x00000100
00696	    CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT = 0x00000400
00697	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE = 0x00002000
00698	    JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK = 0x01000000
00699	    JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION = 9
00700	    JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST = 3
00701	    WAIT_OBJECT_0 = 0
00702	    WAIT_TIMEOUT = 0x00000102
00703	    HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT = 0x00000001
00704	    PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST = 0x00020002
00705	    GENERIC_READ = 0x80000000
00706	    FILE_SHARE_READ = 0x00000001
00707	    FILE_SHARE_WRITE = 0x00000002
00708	    OPEN_EXISTING = 3
00709	    FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL = 0x00000080
00710	    ERROR_MORE_DATA = 234
00711	    ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE = 109
00712	    ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED = 233
00713	    ERROR_HANDLE_EOF = 38
00714	    ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED = 995
00715	    ERROR_NOT_FOUND = 1168
00716	    DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS = 0x00000002
00717	
00718	    # -- the ownership states of one retained handle ----------------------
00719	    #: The façade still owns the handle and nobody else may take it.
00720	    API_OWNED = 'API_OWNED'
00721	    #: The recovery of the façade has **claimed** the handle and it is
00722	    #: preparing to close it.  A handover may not take it and a second
00723	    #: recovery may not claim it again while the claim is alive.
00724	    RECOVERY_CLAIMED = 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'
00725	    #: The handover of the handle is running: the receiver registers it
00726	    #: and the façade forgets it in one atomic step.
00727	    TRANSFER_PENDING = 'TRANSFER_PENDING'
00728	    #: The handle was handed over to its new owner.  It is not in the
00729	    #: holding table any more and it is never closed by the recovery.
00730	    TRANSFERRED = 'TRANSFERRED'
00731	    #: The handle was closed with a confirmed close.
00732	    CLOSED = 'CLOSED'
00733	
00734	    def __init__(self, table=None):
00735	        """The typed façade of the Windows primitives.
00736	
00737	        The table is the primitive table of the broker.  A table may be
00738	        handed in for the inspection of the declaration and of the
00739	        layout: the table of a real run is the one that loads the
00740	        native library, and the table of an inspection never loads one.
00741	        The code that uses the façade is the same code in both cases.
00742	        """
00743	        self._api = table if table is not None else _load_windows_api()
00744	        # The invalid value of a handle has the width of a pointer: it is
00745	        # ``-1`` as a 32 bit value and it is ``0xffffffffffffffff`` as a
00746	        # 64 bit one.  A comparison with the Python value ``-1`` would
00747	        # never match the 64 bit value.
00748	        self.invalid_handle_value = self._api.types['ctypes'].c_void_p(-1).value
00749	        # The holding table of the façade.  Every original handle that a
00750	        # successful ``CreateProcess`` produced enters this table in the
00751	        # moment of its creation, before anything else may fail.  A handle
00752	        # that was handed over to its owner leaves the table; a handle
00753	        # that could not be handed over stays in it, with its owner, its
00754	        # pid and the failures of its release.  The table is the recovery
00755	        # entry point of the façade: the core keeps the façade and it can
00756	        # therefore always reach the handles that it never received.
00757	        self._retained = {}
00758	        self._retained_order = []
00759	        # The one lock of the holding table.  Every mutation of the
00760	        # table and every handover of a handle out of it happen under
00761	        # this lock: a handle that is registered by its receiver and
00762	        # that the façade still names would have **two** owners, and a
00763	        # handle that left the façade before its receiver registered it
00764	        # would have none.
00765	        self._table_lock = threading.RLock()
00766	        # The handovers that really happened.  The list is the evidence
00767	        # of the unique owner of a handle: a handle that was handed
00768	        # over is not in the holding table any more and it is never
00769	        # closed by the recovery of the façade.
00770	        self._handovers = []
00771	        # The ONE deadline of the recovery of the façade.  It is
00772	        # published once: a second recovery of the same round reuses
00773	        # the very same value and it never opens a new budget.  It may
00774	        # only be **shortened** by a context that arrived later.
00775	        self._recovery_deadline = None
00776	        # The absolute deadline of the round, when the façade is bound to
00777	        # one.  Every wait of the façade is bounded by it: a wait that
00778	        # does not fit into the deadline of the round never starts.
00779	        self._deadline = None
00780	        # The ONE context of the round.  The façade reads the absolute
00781	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
00782	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
00783	        self._context = None
00784	        self._clock = time.monotonic
00785	
00786	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline, clock=None):
00787	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round to this façade.
00788	
00789	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
00790	        bound.  The waits of the façade - the wait of a release and the
00791	        wait of the recovery - are bounded by it and they never start a
00792	        wait with a fresh timeout of their own.
00793	        """
00794	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
00795	        if value is None:
00796	            return self._deadline
00797	        if clock is not None:
00798	            self._clock = clock
00799	        if self._deadline is None or value < self._deadline:
00800	            self._deadline = value
00801	        return self._deadline
00802	
00803	    def bind_context(self, context):
00804	        """Bind the ONE context of the round to this façade.
00805	
00806	        The façade reads the absolute deadline and the published
00807	        cleanup deadline of the round from the context and it never
00808	        keeps a budget of its own: a recovery that opened a fresh
00809	        budget of its own would outlive the round that it recovers.
00810	        """
00811	        if context is not None:
00812	            self._context = context
00813	        return self._context
00814	
00815	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
00816	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this façade really uses.
00817	
00818	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
00819	        façade, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
00820	        cleanup deadline of the round are one value: the earliest of
00821	        the four.  The value is recomputed on every call, so a deadline
00822	        that was published after the wait started still shortens it.
00823	        """
00824	        value = _require_deadline(call)
00825	        bound = self._deadline
00826	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
00827	            value = bound
00828	        context = self._context
00829	        if context is not None:
00830	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
00831	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
00832	                value = absolute
00833	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
00834	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
00835	                value = cleanup
00836	        return value
00837	
00838	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
00839	        """The milliseconds that are left until the bound deadline.
00840	
00841	        A façade without a bound deadline keeps the closed wait of the
00842	        package.  A façade with one never waits longer than the deadline
00843	        of the round allows.
00844	        """
00845	        value = self._effective_deadline()
00846	        if value is None:
00847	            return WAIT_MILLISECONDS
00848	        remaining = value - self._clock()
00849	        if remaining <= 0:
00850	            return 0
00851	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
00852	
00853	    def _last_error(self):
00854	        """The last error of the last failing primitive of the host.
00855	
00856	        The value is read with ``ctypes.get_last_error()``, which is
00857	        only correct because the module was loaded with
00858	        ``use_last_error=True``.  A host that does not have that
00859	        function at all cannot report a last error and reports zero.
00860	        """
00861	        getter = getattr(self._api.types['ctypes'], 'get_last_error', None)
00862	        if getter is None:
00863	            return 0
00864	        return int(getter())
00865	
00866	    def _security_attributes(self, inherit):
00867	        """The security attributes of one handle.
00868	
00869	        The attributes say that the handle may be inherited.  A handle
00870	        that is created with ``NULL`` security attributes is never
00871	        inheritable, so the handle list of the process attributes would
00872	        name a handle that the child cannot receive.
00873	        """
00874	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
00875	        attributes = self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES']()
00876	        attributes.nLength = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['SECURITY_ATTRIBUTES'])
00877	        attributes.lpSecurityDescriptor = None
00878	        attributes.bInheritHandle = bool(inherit)
00879	        return attributes
00880	
00881	    # -- the holding table of the original handles -------------------------
00882	    def _retain(self, handle, kind, pid=None, stage='CREATED'):
00883	        """Take one original handle into the holding table of the façade.
00884	
00885	        The table is entered in the moment of the creation of the handle
00886	        and it is left only when the handle was closed with a confirmed
00887	        close or when it was handed over to its owner.  A handle that
00888	        stays in the table is a resource that this round still owns and
00889	        it is named with its kind, its pid and its failures.
00890	        """
00891	        key = id(handle)
00892	        with self._table_lock:
00893	            if key not in self._retained:
00894	                self._retained_order.append(key)
00895	            self._retained[key] = {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind, 'pid': pid,
00896	                                   'stage': stage, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
00897	                                   'state': self.API_OWNED,
00898	                                   'failures': []}
00899	            return self._retained[key]
00900	
00901	    def _forget(self, handle):
00902	        """Leave the holding table: the handle is closed or handed over."""
00903	        key = id(handle)
00904	        with self._table_lock:
00905	            if key in self._retained:
00906	                del self._retained[key]
00907	                if key in self._retained_order:
00908	                    self._retained_order.remove(key)
00909	                return True
00910	        return False
00911	
00912	    def forget_handle(self, handle):
00913	        """Leave the holding table, under the lock of the table.
00914	
00915	        The method is the public face of ``_forget``: the receiver of a
00916	        handle uses it when the façade of the primitives does not offer
00917	        the atomic handover.  A façade that offers the handover uses
00918	        that one instead.
00919	        """
00920	        return self._forget(handle)
00921	
00922	    def _note(self, handle, failure):
00923	        """Name one failure of one retained handle."""
00924	        key = id(handle)
00925	        with self._table_lock:
00926	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
00927	            if entry is not None:
00928	                entry['failures'].append(failure)
00929	            return entry
00930	
00931	    def retained_handles(self):
00932	        """The original handles that this façade still holds.
00933	
00934	        The list is a copy: a caller that changes it changes nothing of
00935	        the holding table of the façade.
00936	        """
00937	        with self._table_lock:
00938	            return [dict(self._retained[key]) for key in self._retained_order
00939	                    if key in self._retained]
00940	
00941	    def handovers(self):
00942	        """The handovers that really happened, as a copy of the log."""
00943	        with self._table_lock:
00944	            return [dict(item) for item in self._handovers]
00945	
00946	    def handover(self, entries, *, owner, register):
00947	        """Hand the named handles over to their new owner, in one step.
00948	
00949	        The receiver registers the handles and the façade forgets them
00950	        under **one** lock.  The two facts are one atomic step: a
00951	        handle that the receiver registered and that the façade still
00952	        names would have two independent owners, and the recovery of
00953	        the façade could close a handle that a live reader of the round
00954	        is reading from.  A handle that left the façade before its
00955	        receiver registered it would have no owner at all.
00956	
00957	        The handover and the recovery of the façade are **the same
00958	        ownership decision**.  A handle that the recovery has already
00959	        claimed is refused here: the recovery is preparing to close it
00960	        and a second owner may never appear.  A refused handle stays
00961	        with the façade and it is never registered with the receiver.
00962	
00963	        A registration that fails leaves the handle with the façade:
00964	        the state of the entry is restored and the handle is never
00965	        half registered and closed by two owners at the same time.
00966	
00967	        ``entries`` is the list of ``(handle, kind)`` pairs and
00968	        ``register`` receives it and registers every handle with the
00969	        receiver.  The handover is recorded, so that the evidence of the
00970	        round can name the owner that received the handle.
00971	        """
00972	        items = list(entries)
00973	        refused = []
00974	        with self._table_lock:
00975	            accepted = []
00976	            for handle, kind in items:
00977	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
00978	                if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
00979	                    # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
00980	                    # is preparing to close it: the handover may not
00981	                    # take it and the receiver may never name it.
00982	                    refused.append((handle, kind))
00983	                    continue
00984	                if entry is not None:
00985	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFER_PENDING
00986	                accepted.append((handle, kind))
00987	            try:
00988	                register(accepted)
00989	            except BaseException:
00990	                # The receiver did not register the handles: they stay
00991	                # with the façade and their state is restored.  A half
00992	                # registered handle is never closed by two owners.
00993	                for handle, _kind in accepted:
00994	                    entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
00995	                    if entry is not None:
00996	                        entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
00997	                raise
00998	            for handle, kind in accepted:
00999	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01000	                if entry is not None:
01001	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
01002	                self._forget(handle)
01003	                self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': owner,
01004	                                        'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
01005	        if refused:
01006	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDOVER_CLAIMED',
01007	                              ','.join(repr(handle) for handle, _kind in refused))
01008	        return True
01009	
01010	    def _claim_entry(self, key):
01011	        """Claim one retained entry for the recovery of the façade.
01012	
01013	        The claim is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table:
01014	        an entry that another recovery already claimed is never claimed
01015	        a second time, and an entry that a handover took is never
01016	        claimed at all.  The claim does not hold the lock of the table
01017	        while it waits: the blocking primitive of the recovery runs
01018	        outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
01019	        """
01020	        with self._table_lock:
01021	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
01022	            if entry is None:
01023	                return None
01024	            if entry.get('state') != self.API_OWNED:
01025	                # The entry is claimed by a recovery that is running or
01026	                # it is in the middle of a handover: it is not the owner
01027	                # of this recovery.
01028	                return None
01029	            entry['state'] = self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
01030	            return dict(entry)
01031	
01032	    def _decide_entries(self, entries, state):
01033	        """One table decision for a set of retained entries.
01034	
01035	        The decision is **one atomic step** under the lock of the table
01036	        and it covers **every** named entry at once: the process and
01037	        the thread of one creation are decided together and a decision
01038	        that reserved one of them and refused the other one is never
01039	        taken.
01040	
01041	        The entries that the decision reserved leave the table in the
01042	        state ``state`` and the entries that it refused keep their own
01043	        state.  An entry that is **not** in the table any more is
01044	        refused as well: it was already closed or already handed over
01045	        and it may never be delivered or closed a second time.
01046	
01047	        The lock of the table is **never** held while a blocking
01048	        primitive runs: the decision is taken, the primitive runs
01049	        outside of the lock and the table stays usable.
01050	        """
01051	        reserved = []
01052	        refused = []
01053	        with self._table_lock:
01054	            for handle, kind in entries:
01055	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01056	                if entry is None:
01057	                    refused.append((handle, kind, 'GONE'))
01058	                    continue
01059	                current = entry.get('state')
01060	                if current == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
01061	                    # The recovery of the façade owns this handle and it
01062	                    # is preparing to close it: nobody else may take it.
01063	                    refused.append((handle, kind, 'RECOVERY_CLAIMED'))
01064	                    continue
01065	                if current != self.API_OWNED:
01066	                    refused.append((handle, kind, str(current)))
01067	                    continue
01068	                entry['state'] = state
01069	                reserved.append((handle, kind))
01070	        return reserved, refused
01071	
01072	    def _restore_entries(self, entries, state):
01073	        """Give the reserved entries back to the table of the façade.
01074	
01075	        A decision that could not be completed gives its entries back:
01076	        the entries keep their original reference and a later recovery
01077	        of the round may claim them again.
01078	        """
01079	        with self._table_lock:
01080	            for handle, _kind in entries:
01081	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01082	                if entry is None:
01083	                    continue
01084	                if entry.get('state') == self.TRANSFER_PENDING:
01085	                    entry['state'] = state
01086	        return True
01087	
01088	    def _commit_entries(self, entries):
01089	        """The reserved entries left the table: they are the property of
01090	        their new owner.
01091	
01092	        The handover of the entries is recorded and it happens under the
01093	        lock of the table: a handle that the receiver registered and
01094	        that the façade still names would have two owners.
01095	        """
01096	        with self._table_lock:
01097	            for handle, kind in entries:
01098	                entry = self._retained.get(id(handle))
01099	                if entry is not None:
01100	                    entry['state'] = self.TRANSFERRED
01101	                self._forget(handle)
01102	                self._handovers.append({'kind': kind, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_API',
01103	                                        'handle_repr': repr(handle)})
01104	        return True
01105	
01106	    def _release_claim(self, key):
01107	        """Give a claim back: a later recovery may claim the entry again."""
01108	        with self._table_lock:
01109	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
01110	            if entry is not None and entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED:
01111	                entry['state'] = self.API_OWNED
01112	
01113	    def _owns_claim(self, key):
01114	        """Whether the claim of this recovery is still the owner of the entry."""
01115	        with self._table_lock:
01116	            entry = self._retained.get(key)
01117	            if entry is None:
01118	                return False
01119	            return entry.get('state') == self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED
01120	
01121	    def _recovery_timeout_ms(self):
01122	        """The milliseconds that are left until the ONE recovery deadline.
01123	
01124	        The deadline of the recovery is published **once**: a second
01125	        recovery of the same round reuses the very same value and it
01126	        never opens a new budget of its own.  It may only be **shortened**
01127	        by a context that arrived later: a recovery that re-read the
01128	        context of the round may not use the long lease of the round
01129	        after the round published its cleanup deadline.
01130	
01131	        A wait of a recovery that arrives after the deadline never
01132	        starts.
01133	        """
01134	        with self._table_lock:
01135	            current = self._effective_deadline()
01136	            if self._recovery_deadline is None:
01137	                value = current
01138	                if value is None:
01139	                    value = self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0)
01140	                self._recovery_deadline = min(
01141	                    value, self._clock() + (WAIT_MILLISECONDS / 1000.0))
01142	            elif current is not None and current < self._recovery_deadline:
01143	                self._recovery_deadline = current
01144	            remaining = self._recovery_deadline - self._clock()
01145	        if remaining <= 0:
01146	            return 0
01147	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
01148	
01149	    def _confirm_process_exit(self, handle, failures, cancellations,
01150	                              timeout_ms=None):
01151	        """Try to confirm the exit of one process, once.
01152	
01153	        The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close
01154	        of a handle are two different facts.  A wait that timed out is
01155	        **not** an exit: the process may still be alive and it may even
01156	        be a suspended process that never ran.  Its control handle is
01157	        the only handle that may stop it.
01158	
01159	        The method returns ``True`` only when the exit was really
01160	        confirmed.  A cancellation of the wait is named and it is handed
01161	        to the caller, which raises it after every safe step was
01162	        executed.
01163	        """
01164	        if timeout_ms is None:
01165	            timeout_ms = self._wait_timeout_ms()
01166	        if timeout_ms <= 0:
01167	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:no_budget')
01168	            return False
01169	        try:
01170	            result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(handle, timeout_ms)
01171	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01172	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:CANCELLED')
01173	            cancellations.append(error)
01174	            return False
01175	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01176	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:' + type(error).__name__)
01177	            return False
01178	        if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
01179	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exited')
01180	            return True
01181	        if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
01182	            failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:exit_not_confirmed')
01183	            return False
01184	        failures.append('WaitForSingleObject:wait=%d' % int(result))
01185	        return False
01186	
01187	    def release_retained(self):
01188	        """Try to release every handle that the façade still holds.
01189	
01190	        This is the recovery entry point of the façade.  It is explicit:
01191	        a caller that wants the handles of a failed creation released
01192	        asks for it, and the result is named.  A handle that could not
01193	        be closed stays in the holding table.
01194	
01195	        Every handle is released on its own and every step of one handle
01196	        is independent of the others: a cancellation in the middle of the
01197	        recovery never skips the close of the next handle.  The
01198	        cancellation is raised at the end of the recovery, after every
01199	        safe step was executed, and it never replaces an ordinary
01200	        failure of a handle.
01201	
01202	        The **control handle of a process** is released only when its
01203	        exit was really confirmed.  A process whose exit was not
01204	        confirmed may still be alive and its control handle is the only
01205	        handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays in the
01206	        holding table with its owner and the recovery of the façade can
01207	        reach it again: a later recovery that confirms the exit closes
01208	        it and forgets it.  A close that succeeds is never a proof that
01209	        the process ended.
01210	
01211	        The recovery and the handover are **one ownership decision**.
01212	        Every entry is **claimed** under the lock of the table before
01213	        the recovery touches it, and a claim that is alive keeps every
01214	        other recovery and every handover away from the entry.  The
01215	        blocking primitive of the recovery runs **outside** of the lock
01216	        of the table: the table is never held while the recovery waits.
01217	        The claim is verified again before the close: an entry that
01218	        changed its owner in the meantime is never closed.
01219	        """
01220	        report = []
01221	        cancellations = []
01222	        for key in list(self._retained_order):
01223	            claimed = self._claim_entry(key)
01224	            if claimed is None:
01225	                # The entry is not owned by this recovery: it is claimed
01226	                # by a recovery that is running or it was handed over.
01227	                # It is never closed here.
01228	                entry = self._retained.get(key)
01229	                if entry is None:
01230	                    continue
01231	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01232	                               'released': False,
01233	                               'failures': ['entry_not_claimed:' + str(
01234	                                   entry.get('state'))]})
01235	                continue
01236	            entry = claimed
01237	            handle = entry['handle']
01238	            failures = []
01239	            try:
01240	                if entry['kind'] == 'process':
01241	                    try:
01242	                        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(handle, 1):
01243	                            failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
01244	                                            % self._last_error())
01245	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01246	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
01247	                        cancellations.append(error)
01248	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01249	                        failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
01250	                    confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(
01251	                        handle, failures, cancellations,
01252	                        timeout_ms=self._recovery_timeout_ms())
01253	                    if not confirmed:
01254	                        # The process may still be alive: the control
01255	                        # handle stays with the façade and it is never
01256	                        # closed under a process that nobody may stop
01257	                        # any more.
01258	                        failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:'
01259	                                        'exit_not_confirmed')
01260	                        self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
01261	                        report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01262	                                       'released': False,
01263	                                       'failures': list(failures)})
01264	                        continue
01265	                # The claim is verified again before the close: an entry
01266	                # that changed its owner while the recovery waited is
01267	                # never closed by this recovery.
01268	                if not self._owns_claim(key):
01269	                    failures.append('entry_claim_lost:before_close')
01270	                    report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01271	                                   'released': False,
01272	                                   'failures': list(failures)})
01273	                    continue
01274	                if self._close_quietly(handle, failures, cancellations):
01275	                    self._forget(handle)
01276	                else:
01277	                    self._note(handle, '|'.join(failures))
01278	                report.append({'kind': entry['kind'], 'pid': entry['pid'],
01279	                               'released': not any(failure.startswith('CloseHandle')
01280	                                                   for failure in failures),
01281	                               'failures': list(failures)})
01282	            finally:
01283	                # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is
01284	                # given back: a later recovery of the round may claim
01285	                # the entry again and it may confirm the exit.
01286	                self._release_claim(key)
01287	        if cancellations:
01288	            # The cancellation is raised after every safe step of the
01289	            # recovery was executed.  It is never swallowed and it never
01290	            # replaces an ordinary failure that is named in the report.
01291	            raise cancellations[0]
01292	        return report
01293	
01294	    # -- the job object ---------------------------------------------------
01295	    def create_job(self):
01296	        handle = self._api.kernel32.CreateJobObjectW(None, None)
01297	        if not handle:
01298	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_CREATE_FAILED',
01299	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01300	        return handle
01301	
01302	    def set_job_kill_on_close(self, job):
01303	        """Set the kill-on-close limit of the job and nothing else.
01304	
01305	        ``JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_BREAKAWAY_OK`` is deliberately *not* set: a
01306	        child of the round may not break away from the job.
01307	        """
01308	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01309	        limit = self._api.JOBOBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION()
01310	        limit.BasicLimitInformation.LimitFlags = self.JOB_OBJECT_LIMIT_KILL_ON_JOB_CLOSE
01311	        if not self._api.kernel32.SetInformationJobObject(
01312	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_EXTENDED_LIMIT_INFORMATION,
01313	                ctypes.byref(limit), ctypes.sizeof(limit)):
01314	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_LIMIT_FAILED',
01315	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01316	        return True
01317	
01318	    def terminate_job(self, job, exit_code):
01319	        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateJobObject(job, exit_code):
01320	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
01321	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01322	        return True
01323	
01324	    def job_process_ids(self, job):
01325	        """The bounded list of the process ids of the job.
01326	
01327	        The query is bounded by the descendant cap of this package.  A
01328	        job that holds more processes than the cap is *not* reported as an
01329	        empty job: the answer is unknown, and an unknown answer never
01330	        becomes a zero.
01331	
01332	        The structure of Windows holds two counts and an **inline** array
01333	        of ``ULONG_PTR``.  The array is read from the buffer at the
01334	        offset of the array and never through a pointer field.
01335	
01336	        The answer is only accepted when **every** fact of it agrees:
01337	
01338	        * the returned length is at least the size of the header, so that
01339	          the two counts were really written and a legal empty job still
01340	          carries its complete header;
01341	        * the returned length is at most the size of the buffer, so that
01342	          a length that is larger than the buffer was never accepted;
01343	        * the returned length is at least the length that the count of
01344	          the list needs, so that the list was not truncated;
01345	        * the two counts are equal, so that the number of the assigned
01346	          processes and the number of the listed processes agree;
01347	        * the count of the list is within the cap of this package.
01348	
01349	        Every other result is **unknown**: an exception, a returned
01350	        length of zero, a reversed pair of counts, a count over the cap
01351	        and a truncated list.  An unknown answer is never an empty job
01352	        and never a success.
01353	        """
01354	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01355	        capacity = MAX_DESCENDANTS + 1
01356	        pointer_size = ctypes.sizeof(ctypes.c_size_t)
01357	        header_size = 2 * ctypes.sizeof(self._api.types['DWORD'])
01358	        size = header_size + capacity * pointer_size
01359	        buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
01360	        returned = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01361	        if not self._api.kernel32.QueryInformationJobObject(
01362	                job, self.JOB_OBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST, buffer, size,
01363	                ctypes.byref(returned)):
01364	            error = self._last_error()
01365	            if error == self.ERROR_MORE_DATA:
01366	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01367	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_UNKNOWN', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01368	        header = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(
01369	            self._api.JOBOBJECT_BASIC_PROCESS_ID_LIST)).contents
01370	        assigned = int(header.NumberOfAssignedProcesses)
01371	        listed = int(header.NumberOfProcessIdsInList)
01372	        reported = int(returned.value)
01373	        # The returned length has to carry the whole header and it may
01374	        # never be larger than the buffer that was handed to Windows.
01375	        if reported < header_size or reported > size:
01376	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01377	                              'returned=%d:header=%d:allocated=%d'
01378	                              % (reported, header_size, size))
01379	        if assigned > capacity or listed > capacity:
01380	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
01381	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01382	        if assigned != listed:
01383	            # The two counts disagree: the answer is unknown and it is
01384	            # never an empty job.
01385	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01386	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01387	        if listed > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
01388	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT',
01389	                              'assigned=%d:listed=%d' % (assigned, listed))
01390	        needed = header_size + listed * pointer_size
01391	        if needed > reported:
01392	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_JOB_QUERY_TRUNCATED',
01393	                              'returned=%d:needed=%d' % (reported, needed))
01394	        if listed == 0:
01395	            # A legal empty job: the header was complete and the two
01396	            # counts were zero.
01397	            return []
01398	        offset = header_size // pointer_size
01399	        identifiers = ctypes.cast(buffer, ctypes.POINTER(ctypes.c_size_t))
01400	        return [int(identifiers[offset + index]) for index in range(listed)]
01401	
01402	    # -- the processes -----------------------------------------------------
01403	    def create_process_suspended(self, *, executable, arguments, working_directory,
01404	                                  stdout_handle, stderr_handle, environment=None):
01405	        """Create one suspended process and return its original handles.
01406	
01407	        The child inherits exactly three handles: the write end of its
01408	        standard output, the write end of its standard error and the null
01409	        device as its standard input.  The inheritance set is passed
01410	        explicitly as the handle list of the process attributes, so that
01411	        the control channel of the broker, the job handle and every other
01412	        handle of the broker stay in the broker.
01413	
01414	        The size of the start up information is the size of the whole
01415	        ``STARTUPINFOEXW``, because the extended flag is set and the
01416	        attribute list follows the start up information inside it.
01417	
01418	        The creation has **independent** steps.  The deletion of the
01419	        attribute list, the close of the null device, the birth query of
01420	        the created process and the release of a process that could not
01421	        be adopted do not depend on each other: a failure of one of them
01422	        never skips the others.  The first error of the creation is the
01423	        error that leaves the method and the later errors are named next
01424	        to it; the first error is never replaced by a later one.
01425	
01426	        The original handles of a created process enter the holding table
01427	        of this façade in the moment of the creation.  The null device
01428	        of the child enters it in the moment of its creation as well: a
01429	        null device that could not be closed is a resource of this round
01430	        and it stays with its owner.  A handle that was handed over to
01431	        the caller leaves the table; a handle that could not be closed
01432	        stays in it with its owner and its failures, so that the recovery
01433	        of the façade can always reach it.
01434	        """
01435	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01436	        kernel32 = self._api.kernel32
01437	        failures = []
01438	        command = ctypes.create_unicode_buffer(_windows_command_line(executable, arguments))
01439	        environment_block = _environment_block(environment)
01440	        attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
01441	        null = kernel32.CreateFileW('NUL', self.GENERIC_READ,
01442	                                    self.FILE_SHARE_READ | self.FILE_SHARE_WRITE,
01443	                                    ctypes.byref(attributes), self.OPEN_EXISTING,
01444	                                    self.FILE_ATTRIBUTE_NORMAL, None)
01445	        if not null or null == self.invalid_handle_value:
01446	            error = self._last_error()
01447	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_FAILED',
01448	                              'last_error=%d' % error)
01449	        # The moment of the creation of the null device.  The handle of
01450	        # the null device enters the holding table of this façade right
01451	        # here: a null device whose close fails later is a resource of
01452	        # this round and it keeps its original owner and its recovery
01453	        # entry point.
01454	        self._retain(null, 'null_device')
01455	        attribute_list = None
01456	        information = None
01457	        process = None
01458	        thread = None
01459	        pid = None
01460	        primary = None
01461	        cleanup_error = None
01462	        cancellations = []
01463	        try:
01464	            try:
01465	                inherited = (ctypes.c_void_p * 3)(stdout_handle, stderr_handle, null)
01466	                size = ctypes.c_size_t(0)
01467	                kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(None, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size))
01468	                attribute_list = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size.value)
01469	                if not kernel32.InitializeProcThreadAttributeList(
01470	                        attribute_list, 1, 0, ctypes.byref(size)):
01471	                    error = self._last_error()
01472	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
01473	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
01474	                if not kernel32.UpdateProcThreadAttribute(
01475	                        attribute_list, 0, self.PROC_THREAD_ATTRIBUTE_HANDLE_LIST,
01476	                        inherited, ctypes.sizeof(inherited), None, None):
01477	                    error = self._last_error()
01478	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_FAILED',
01479	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
01480	                startup = self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW()
01481	                # The extended start up information is passed, so the
01482	                # size is the size of the whole structure and not the
01483	                # size of the plain start up information.
01484	                startup.StartupInfo.cb = ctypes.sizeof(self._api.STARTUPINFOEXW)
01485	                startup.StartupInfo.dwFlags = self.STARTF_USESTDHANDLES
01486	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdInput = null
01487	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdOutput = stdout_handle
01488	                startup.StartupInfo.hStdError = stderr_handle
01489	                startup.lpAttributeList = ctypes.cast(attribute_list, ctypes.c_void_p)
01490	                information = self._api.PROCESS_INFORMATION()
01491	                flags = (self.CREATE_SUSPENDED | self.EXTENDED_STARTUPINFO_PRESENT
01492	                         | self.CREATE_UNICODE_ENVIRONMENT)
01493	                environment_pointer = (ctypes.cast(environment_block, ctypes.c_void_p)
01494	                                       if environment_block is not None else None)
01495	                created = kernel32.CreateProcessW(
01496	                    str(executable), command, None, None, True, flags,
01497	                    environment_pointer, str(working_directory),
01498	                    ctypes.byref(startup.StartupInfo), ctypes.byref(information))
01499	                if not created:
01500	                    error = self._last_error()
01501	                    raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_CREATE_FAILED',
01502	                                      'last_error=%d' % error)
01503	                # The moment of the creation.  The original handles of
01504	                # the process enter the holding table of this façade
01505	                # before anything else may fail: they are never only an
01506	                # integer in a local list or in the detail of an error.
01507	                process = information.hProcess
01508	                thread = information.hThread
01509	                pid = int(information.dwProcessId)
01510	                self._retain(process, 'process', pid)
01511	                self._retain(thread, 'thread', pid)
01512	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01513	                primary = error
01514	            finally:
01515	                # The cleanups of the creation are independent of each
01516	                # other and independent of the failure that is on the way
01517	                # out.  A cleanup that fails never skips the other one and
01518	                # a cancellation in the middle of a cleanup never skips
01519	                # the cleanup that follows it: the cancellation is named
01520	                # and it is raised after every safe step was executed.
01521	                attribute_failures = []
01522	                if attribute_list is not None:
01523	                    try:
01524	                        kernel32.DeleteProcThreadAttributeList(attribute_list)
01525	                    except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01526	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:CANCELLED')
01527	                        cancellations.append(error)
01528	                    except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01529	                        attribute_failures.append('DeleteProcThreadAttributeList:'
01530	                                                  + type(error).__name__)
01531	                null_failures = []
01532	                if self._close_quietly(null, null_failures, cancellations):
01533	                    # The null device was closed with a confirmed close:
01534	                    # it may be forgotten.
01535	                    self._forget(null)
01536	                failures.extend(attribute_failures)
01537	                failures.extend(null_failures)
01538	                if attribute_failures:
01539	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_ATTRIBUTE_LIST_CLEANUP_FAILED',
01540	                                                '|'.join(attribute_failures))
01541	                elif null_failures:
01542	                    cleanup_error = BrokerError('BROKER_NULL_DEVICE_CLOSE_FAILED',
01543	                                                '|'.join(null_failures))
01544	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01545	            if primary is None:
01546	                primary = error
01547	        if cancellations and primary is None:
01548	            # The cancellation of a cleanup is the primary error of the
01549	            # creation when nothing else failed.  The cleanups that were
01550	            # safe were executed before it was raised and the ordinary
01551	            # failures that were named stay named next to it.
01552	            primary = cancellations[0]
01553	            failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
01554	        creation = None
01555	        if process is not None:
01556	            # The birth query is the identity of the created process.  It
01557	            # is independent of the cleanups above: it always runs when a
01558	            # process was created.
01559	            try:
01560	                creation = self._process_creation_time(process)
01561	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01562	                if primary is None:
01563	                    primary = error
01564	        if primary is None and cleanup_error is None:
01565	            # The creation is handed over to the caller: the original
01566	            # handles are the property of the caller now and they leave
01567	            # the holding table.  The handover is **one** decision of
01568	            # the table: the process and the thread are reserved
01569	            # together, and a handle that the recovery of the façade
01570	            # claimed is never delivered and never forgotten here.
01571	            reserved, refused = self._decide_entries(
01572	                [(process, 'process'), (thread, 'thread')],
01573	                self.TRANSFER_PENDING)
01574	            if not refused:
01575	                self._commit_entries(reserved)
01576	                return CreatedProcess(process, thread, pid, creation, failures)
01577	            # The recovery of the façade owns one of the original
01578	            # handles: the creation may not deliver a stale handle to
01579	            # the core.  The creation failed and the direct cleanup
01580	            # below claims the entries that it may claim.
01581	            self._restore_entries(reserved, self.API_OWNED)
01582	            primary = BrokerError(
01583	                'BROKER_PROCESS_HANDOVER_REFUSED',
01584	                ','.join('%s:%s' % (kind, reason)
01585	                         for _handle, kind, reason in refused))
01586	        # The creation failed.  A process that was created is released
01587	        # through its own original handle and every failure is named.
01588	        release_failures = []
01589	        if process is not None:
01590	            self._release_created(process, thread, release_failures, cancellations)
01591	        failures.extend(release_failures)
01592	        if cancellations and primary is None:
01593	            # The cancellation of the release is the primary error of the
01594	            # creation when nothing else failed.  Every safe step was
01595	            # executed before it is raised.
01596	            primary = cancellations[0]
01597	            failures.append('creation_cancelled:' + type(primary).__name__)
