01598	        error = primary if primary is not None else cleanup_error
01599	        detail = getattr(error, 'detail', None)
01600	        if failures:
01601	            error.detail = ((detail or '') + '|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01602	        if cancellations and error is not cancellations[0]:
01603	            # The primary error and the cancellation coexist: the
01604	            # cancellation object stays reachable next to the primary
01605	            # error and it is never replaced by a copy of a message.
01606	            try:
01607	                error.lvw_cancelled = cancellations[0]
01608	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01609	                pass
01610	        raise error
01611	
01612	    def _close_quietly(self, handle, failures, cancellations=None):
01613	        """Close one handle and name a failure of the close.
01614	
01615	        A cancellation in the middle of a close never skips the close
01616	        that follows it: it is named in the failures and it is handed to
01617	        the caller, which raises it after every safe step was executed.
01618	        """
01619	        try:
01620	            if not self._api.kernel32.CloseHandle(handle):
01621	                failures.append('CloseHandle:last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01622	                return False
01623	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01624	            failures.append('CloseHandle:CANCELLED')
01625	            if cancellations is not None:
01626	                cancellations.append(error)
01627	            return False
01628	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01629	            failures.append('CloseHandle:' + type(error).__name__)
01630	            return False
01631	        return True
01632	
01633	    def _release_created(self, process, thread, failures, cancellations=None):
01634	        """Release a process that was created but never adopted.
01635	
01636	        The process is stopped through its own original handle, its exit
01637	        is confirmed and both of its handles are closed.  A failure of
01638	        one of the steps is named and the other steps still run: a handle
01639	        is never left to the garbage collector.  A cancellation in the
01640	        middle of the release never skips the close that follows it: it
01641	        is named and the release continues.
01642	
01643	        The direct cleanup of a failed creation is **one** decision of
01644	        the table, exactly like the recovery of the façade and exactly
01645	        like the handover of a pipe: the original entries are claimed
01646	        before they are touched, the decision covers the process and
01647	        the thread together and a handle that the recovery of the
01648	        façade already claimed is **never** closed here.  The claim is
01649	        taken under the lock of the table and the blocking primitives
01650	        of the release run **outside** of it.
01651	
01652	        The confirmation of the exit and the confirmation of the close of
01653	        a handle are two separate facts and they are recorded separately:
01654	        a wait that timed out is not an exit and a close that failed is
01655	        not a close.  A handle that could not be closed stays in the
01656	        holding table of the façade: the original reference is kept and
01657	        the recovery of the façade can reach it.
01658	
01659	        The **control handle of the process** is the one exception: it
01660	        is closed only when the exit was really confirmed.  A process
01661	        whose exit was not confirmed may still be alive - it may be a
01662	        suspended process that never ran - and its control handle is the
01663	        only handle that may stop it.  The handle stays in the holding
01664	        table of the façade with its owner, so that the recovery of the
01665	        façade can reach it again.
01666	
01667	        The cancellations of the release are collected in the list of
01668	        the caller: they are raised after every safe step was executed
01669	        and they never replace the primary error of the creation.
01670	        """
01671	        if cancellations is None:
01672	            cancellations = []
01673	        entries = [(process, 'process')]
01674	        if thread is not None:
01675	            entries.append((thread, 'thread'))
01676	        claimed, refused = self._decide_entries(entries, self.RECOVERY_CLAIMED)
01677	        for _handle, kind, reason in refused:
01678	            failures.append('create_release_refused:%s:%s' % (kind, reason))
01679	        owned = {id(handle) for handle, _kind in claimed}
01680	        closed = set()
01681	        if id(process) in owned:
01682	            try:
01683	                if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(process, 1):
01684	                    failures.append('TerminateProcess:last_error=%d'
01685	                                    % self._last_error())
01686	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01687	                failures.append('TerminateProcess:CANCELLED')
01688	                cancellations.append(error)
01689	            except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01690	                failures.append('TerminateProcess:' + type(error).__name__)
01691	            confirmed = self._confirm_process_exit(process, failures, cancellations)
01692	            if not confirmed:
01693	                # The process may still be alive.  Its control handle
01694	                # stays with the façade: it is never closed and never
01695	                # forgotten.
01696	                failures.append('process_control_handle_retained:exit_not_confirmed')
01697	                self._note(process, 'exit_not_confirmed:control_handle_retained')
01698	            elif self._close_quietly(process, failures, cancellations):
01699	                self._forget(process)
01700	                closed.add(id(process))
01701	            else:
01702	                self._note(process, 'close_not_confirmed')
01703	        # The thread handle is independent of the exit of the process: it
01704	        # is closed on its own and it never waits for the process.
01705	        if thread is not None and id(thread) in owned:
01706	            if self._close_quietly(thread, failures, cancellations):
01707	                self._forget(thread)
01708	                closed.add(id(thread))
01709	            else:
01710	                self._note(thread, 'close_not_confirmed')
01711	        # A claim that did not end with a confirmed close is given back:
01712	        # a later recovery of the round may claim the entry again and it
01713	        # may confirm the exit.
01714	        for handle, _kind in claimed:
01715	            if id(handle) not in closed:
01716	                self._release_claim(id(handle))
01717	        if cancellations:
01718	            failures.append('release_cancelled:' + type(cancellations[0]).__name__)
01719	        return failures
01720	
01721	    def _process_creation_time(self, process):
01722	        """The birth time of one process, as the 100 nanosecond tick count.
01723	
01724	        The query is the identity of the process: a process whose birth
01725	        time cannot be read has no proven identity and is not used.
01726	        """
01727	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01728	        creation = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01729	        exit_time = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01730	        kernel = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01731	        user = self._api.types['FILETIME']()
01732	        if not self._api.kernel32.GetProcessTimes(
01733	                process, ctypes.byref(creation), ctypes.byref(exit_time),
01734	                ctypes.byref(kernel), ctypes.byref(user)):
01735	            error = self._last_error()
01736	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PROCESS_BIRTH_QUERY_FAILED',
01737	                              'last_error=%d' % error)
01738	        return (int(creation.dwHighDateTime) << 32) | int(creation.dwLowDateTime)
01739	
01740	    def assign_process_to_job(self, job, process):
01741	        if not self._api.kernel32.AssignProcessToJobObject(job, process):
01742	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_ASSIGN_FAILED',
01743	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01744	        return True
01745	
01746	    def resume_thread(self, thread):
01747	        result = self._api.kernel32.ResumeThread(thread)
01748	        if result == 0xFFFFFFFF:
01749	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_RESUME_FAILED',
01750	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01751	        return int(result)
01752	
01753	    def terminate_process(self, process, exit_code):
01754	        if not self._api.kernel32.TerminateProcess(process, exit_code):
01755	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_TERMINATE_FAILED',
01756	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01757	        return True
01758	
01759	    def wait_process(self, process, timeout_ms):
01760	        result = self._api.kernel32.WaitForSingleObject(process, timeout_ms)
01761	        if result == self.WAIT_OBJECT_0:
01762	            return True
01763	        if result == self.WAIT_TIMEOUT:
01764	            return False
01765	        raise BrokerError('BROKER_WAIT_FAILED', 'wait=%d' % int(result))
01766	
01767	    def exit_code(self, process):
01768	        code = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01769	        if not self._api.kernel32.GetExitCodeProcess(
01770	                process, self._api.types['ctypes'].byref(code)):
01771	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_EXIT_CODE_FAILED',
01772	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01773	        return int(code.value)
01774	
01775	    def close_handle(self, handle):
01776	        if not self._api.kernel32.CloseHandle(handle):
01777	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED',
01778	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01779	        # The close was confirmed: the handle leaves the holding table
01780	        # of the façade.  A handle that could not be closed stays in it
01781	        # with its owner and it is reachable by the recovery.
01782	        self._forget(handle)
01783	        return True
01784	
01785	    # -- the pipes ---------------------------------------------------------
01786	    def create_pipe(self, name):
01787	        """Create one pipe.  Only the write end is inheritable.
01788	
01789	        The security attributes of the pipe say that the ends are
01790	        inheritable and the inherit flag of the *read* end is cleared
01791	        right afterwards.  A pipe that was created with ``NULL``
01792	        security attributes can never be inherited at all.
01793	
01794	        Both ends enter the holding table of this façade in the moment
01795	        of their creation.  A pipe whose inherit flag could not be
01796	        cleared therefore keeps its original ends with their owner: the
01797	        core never received the pair and the round never loses the
01798	        ownership of a handle that it created.
01799	
01800	        The failure of the inherit flag is cleaned up **independently**
01801	        of the two ends.  A cancellation of the inherit flag and a
01802	        cancellation of the close of one end never skip the close of the
01803	        other end: the safe close of both ends always runs and the
01804	        cancellation is raised afterwards.  The end that could not be
01805	        closed keeps its original reference with the façade and the end
01806	        that was closed with a confirmed close leaves the table.
01807	        """
01808	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01809	        read = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01810	        write = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01811	        attributes = self._security_attributes(True)
01812	        if not self._api.kernel32.CreatePipe(ctypes.byref(read), ctypes.byref(write),
01813	                                             ctypes.byref(attributes), 0):
01814	            error = self._last_error()
01815	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_FAILED', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01816	        # The moment of the creation of the two ends.
01817	        self._retain(read, 'pipe_read')
01818	        self._retain(write, 'pipe_write')
01819	        primary = None
01820	        inherited = False
01821	        try:
01822	            inherited = bool(self._api.kernel32.SetHandleInformation(
01823	                read, self.HANDLE_FLAG_INHERIT, 0))
01824	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
01825	            # The cancellation of the inherit flag is the primary error
01826	            # of the pipe and it never skips the safe close of the ends.
01827	            primary = error
01828	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01829	            primary = BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED',
01830	                                  'last_error=%d:%s' % (self._last_error(),
01831	                                                        type(error).__name__))
01832	        if inherited:
01833	            return read, write
01834	        error = self._last_error()
01835	        failures = []
01836	        cancellations = []
01837	        if self._close_quietly(read, failures, cancellations):
01838	            self._forget(read)
01839	        if self._close_quietly(write, failures, cancellations):
01840	            self._forget(write)
01841	        if primary is None:
01842	            primary = BrokerError(
01843	                'BROKER_PIPE_INHERIT_FAILED',
01844	                'last_error=%d%s' % (error,
01845	                                     ('|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01846	                                     if failures else ''))
01847	        else:
01848	            try:
01849	                primary.detail = ((getattr(primary, 'detail', None) or '')
01850	                                  + '|cleanup=' + ','.join(failures))
01851	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01852	                pass
01853	        if cancellations:
01854	            # The cancellation of a close is the error that leaves the
01855	            # method and the primary error stays reachable next to it:
01856	            # a cancellation never becomes an ordinary evidence and it
01857	            # never hides the error that caused the pipe.
01858	            cancel = cancellations[0]
01859	            try:
01860	                cancel.lvw_primary = primary
01861	                primary.lvw_cancelled = cancel
01862	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
01863	                pass
01864	            raise cancel
01865	        raise primary
01866	
01867	    def read_stream(self, handle, size):
01868	        """Read one block of a child stream.
01869	
01870	        The end of a stream is an end and not an error, but only in the
01871	        branches that Windows documents as the end of a pipe: a broken
01872	        pipe, a pipe that is not connected any more, the end of the file
01873	        and an operation that was aborted.  Every other failure of the
01874	        read is a failure of the round and it is named.
01875	        """
01876	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01877	        buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(size)
01878	        read = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01879	        if not self._api.kernel32.ReadFile(handle, buffer, size,
01880	                                           ctypes.byref(read), None):
01881	            error = self._last_error()
01882	            if error in (self.ERROR_BROKEN_PIPE, self.ERROR_PIPE_NOT_CONNECTED,
01883	                         self.ERROR_HANDLE_EOF, self.ERROR_OPERATION_ABORTED):
01884	                return b''
01885	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STREAM_READ_FAILED', 'last_error=%d' % error)
01886	        return buffer.raw[:read.value]
01887	
01888	    def write_stream(self, handle, data):
01889	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01890	        buffer = ctypes.create_string_buffer(bytes(data), len(data))
01891	        written = self._api.types['DWORD'](0)
01892	        if not self._api.kernel32.WriteFile(handle, buffer, len(data),
01893	                                            ctypes.byref(written), None):
01894	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STREAM_WRITE_FAILED',
01895	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01896	        return int(written.value)
01897	
01898	    # -- the cancellation of a synchronous operation -----------------------
01899	    def duplicate_current_thread_handle(self):
01900	        """The original handle of the calling thread.
01901	
01902	        The handle is the handle of the thread itself and never a self
01903	        reported identifier: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` cancels the
01904	        operation of one thread and it needs the real handle of that
01905	        thread.
01906	        """
01907	        ctypes = self._api.types['ctypes']
01908	        thread = self._api.kernel32.GetCurrentThread()
01909	        process = self._api.kernel32.GetCurrentProcess()
01910	        duplicate = self._api.types['HANDLE']()
01911	        if not self._api.kernel32.DuplicateHandle(process, thread, process,
01912	                                                  ctypes.byref(duplicate), 0,
01913	                                                  False, self.DUPLICATE_SAME_ACCESS):
01914	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_THREAD_HANDLE_FAILED',
01915	                              'last_error=%d' % self._last_error())
01916	        return duplicate
01917	
01918	    def cancel_synchronous_io(self, thread_handle):
01919	        """Ask for the cancellation of the synchronous operation of a thread.
01920	
01921	        The call is a **request** and not a completion.  It may report
01922	        ``ERROR_NOT_FOUND`` because the operation had not started yet or
01923	        because it had just finished: the caller has to read the real
01924	        completion of the thread and to join it.  The return value of
01925	        this method names the state of the request.
01926	        """
01927	        if not self._api.kernel32.CancelSynchronousIo(thread_handle):
01928	            error = self._last_error()
01929	            if error == self.ERROR_NOT_FOUND:
01930	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
01931	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCEL_FAILED',
01932	                              'last_error=%d' % error)
01933	        return 'REQUESTED'
01934	
01935	
01936	# -- the fixed command of a role ------------------------------------------
01937	
01938	def _base_environment():
01939	    environment = {key: value for key, value in os.environ.items()
01940	                   if key.upper() not in REMOVED_ENVIRONMENT}
01941	    environment['PYTHONDONTWRITEBYTECODE'] = '1'
01942	    environment['PYTHONNOUSERSITE'] = '1'
01943	    environment['NO_PROXY'] = '127.0.0.1,localhost'
01944	    return environment
01945	
01946	
01947	def _comfy_environment(root):
01948	    environment = _base_environment()
01949	    temporary = str(Path(root) / 'temp' / 'service-os')
01950	    environment.update({
01951	        'HF_HUB_OFFLINE': '1',
01952	        'TRANSFORMERS_OFFLINE': '1',
01953	        'HF_DATASETS_OFFLINE': '1',
01954	        'TEMP': temporary,
01955	        'TMP': temporary,
01956	        'TORCH_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'torch'),
01957	        'HF_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'huggingface'),
01958	        'XDG_CACHE_HOME': str(Path(temporary) / 'cache'),
01959	    })
01960	    return environment
01961	
01962	
01963	def _ollama_environment():
01964	    environment = _base_environment()
01965	    environment['OLLAMA_HOST'] = '127.0.0.1:11434'
01966	    environment['OLLAMA_NO_CLOUD'] = '1'
01967	    return environment
01968	
01969	
01970	def role_command(role, run_root, owned_pids):
01971	    """Assemble the fixed command of one role.
01972	
01973	    The command form of a role is frozen in this module and it is
01974	    assembled from the tools that the plan closed.  The plan never carries
01975	    an argument vector, a shell, a script or an environment: it carries
01976	    the tools and the closed data of the role, and this function is the
01977	    only place where a command line of this package exists.
01978	
01979	    The forms are the ones of the already authorised launch of this pack:
01980	    the ComfyUI launch of ``reports/v0-s1/wp-s1-03-phase-a/live_probe.py``
01981	    and of ``tools/v0_s2_driver.py``, the Ollama launch of the same
01982	    wrapper, and the sampler launch of ``tools/v0_s2_runtime.py``.
01983	
01984	    The private process ids of the sampler are passed as *separate*
01985	    arguments: the parser of the sampler is ``type=int, nargs='+'`` and
01986	    one argument that carries a space would not be an integer.
01987	    """
01988	    name = role['role']
01989	    tools = role['tools']
01990	    data = role['data']
01991	    root = str(run_root)
01992	    if name == 'COMFY':
01993	        python = tools[0]['path']
01994	        main = tools[1]['path']
01995	        arguments = ['-s', '-B', main,
01996	                     '--listen', '127.0.0.1', '--port', '8191',
01997	                     '--base-directory', root,
01998	                     '--user-directory', str(Path(root) / data['user_directory']),
01999	                     '--disable-auto-launch', '--disable-api-nodes',
02000	                     '--disable-all-custom-nodes',
02001	                     '--whitelist-custom-nodes', 'lvw_v0_bridge']
02002	        return {'executable': python, 'arguments': arguments,
02003	                'working_directory': root, 'environment': _comfy_environment(root)}
02004	    if name == 'OLLAMA':
02005	        executable = tools[0]['path']
02006	        return {'executable': executable, 'arguments': ['serve'],
02007	                'working_directory': root, 'environment': _ollama_environment()}
02008	    if name == 'SAMPLER':
02009	        python = tools[0]['path']
02010	        script = tools[1]['path']
02011	        directory = str(Path(root) / 'temp' / 'resource-sampler')
02012	        arguments = ['-I', '-B', script, '--execute-sampler',
02013	                     '--run-root', directory,
02014	                     '--nvidia-smi', data['nvidia_exe'],
02015	                     '--binary-sha256', data['nvidia_sha256'],
02016	                     '--private-pids'] + [str(pid) for pid in owned_pids] + \
02017	                    ['--max-seconds', str(data['max_seconds'])]
02018	        return {'executable': python, 'arguments': arguments,
02019	                'working_directory': directory, 'environment': _base_environment()}
02020	    raise BrokerError('BROKER_ROLE_UNKNOWN', name)
02021	
02022	
02023	# -- the bounded reader of a child stream ----------------------------------
02024	
02025	class _BoundedStreamReader(threading.Thread):
02026	    """One bounded background reader of one stream of a child.
02027	
02028	    The thread only reads.  It never writes, it never calls the broker and
02029	    it never waits for the broker.  The broker takes the published counts
02030	    out of it at its own checkpoints, so the deadline of the round is never
02031	    held up by a silent child.
02032	
02033	    A read that failed for a reason that Windows does not document as the
02034	    end of a pipe is a named error of the round: the reader keeps it and
02035	    the round is not a release.
02036	    """
02037	
02038	    def __init__(self, win32, handle, *, limit, name):
02039	        super().__init__(daemon=True, name='lvw-native-custody-stream-' + name)
02040	        self.win32 = win32
02041	        self.handle = handle
02042	        self.limit = limit
02043	        self.observed = 0
02044	        self.kept = 0
02045	        self.overflow = False
02046	        self.error = None
02047	        self.finished = threading.Event()
02048	
02049	    def run(self):
02050	        try:
02051	            while True:
02052	                block = self.win32.read_stream(self.handle, 65536)
02053	                if not block:
02054	                    break
02055	                self.observed += len(block)
02056	                if self.observed > self.limit:
02057	                    self.overflow = True
02058	                    break
02059	                self.kept += len(block)
02060	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02061	            self.error = getattr(error, 'code', None) or type(error).__name__
02062	        finally:
02063	            self.finished.set()
02064	
02065	
02066	# -- the pending resources of one round ------------------------------------
02067	
02068	def _json_fact(value):
02069	    """One fact of the evidence of the round that is always serialisable.
02070	
02071	    A handle is an integer in the offline validation and an opaque
02072	    object in a real run: the evidence of the round carries a printable
02073	    name of it and it never carries the object itself.  A callable is
02074	    never serialisable either and it is named instead.
02075	    """
02076	    if value is None or isinstance(value, (bool, int, float, str)):
02077	        return value
02078	    return repr(value)
02079	
02080	
02081	class _PendingResources:
02082	    """The pending resource table of one round.
02083	
02084	    Every handle that this round creates enters this table in the moment
02085	    of its successful creation.  A handle **stays** in the table until it
02086	    is closed with a confirmed close: the handover of a handle to its
02087	    owner changes the owner and nothing else.  The handle is a resource
02088	    that this round owns and it is named until it is really closed.
02089	
02090	    A handle that could not be closed stays in the table with its owner:
02091	    it is a resource that this round still owns and it is named in the
02092	    evidence of the round.
02093	
02094	    A handle of another owner is never in this table and it is never
02095	    touched.
02096	
02097	    The table carries the **owner object** of a handle and not only its
02098	    name: a handle that is owned by a live reader is protected from the
02099	    pending pass by the fact that the reader is alive and not by a
02100	    string that somebody may write.  The owner object is the reader
02101	    itself and the table asks it.
02102	    """
02103	
02104	    def __init__(self, win32):
02105	        self._win32 = win32
02106	        self._items = []
02107	        self._lock = threading.RLock()
02108	
02109	    def add(self, handle, kind, *, role=None, owner=None, owner_ref=None,
02110	            closeable=None):
02111	        """Enter one handle into the table of the round.
02112	
02113	        The ``closeable`` predicate is the **close eligibility** of the
02114	        handle.  It is asked by every close of the table and it is the
02115	        authority of the question "may this handle be closed now?": a
02116	        control handle of a process whose exit was not confirmed is
02117	        never closed, and the predicate is the **owner** of that fact
02118	        and not a name of it.
02119	
02120	        The ``owner_ref`` is the object of the owner of the handle: the
02121	        pending pass asks it before it closes the handle.
02122	        """
02123	        item = {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind, 'role': role,
02124	                'owner': owner or 'PENDING', 'owner_ref': owner_ref,
02125	                'closeable': closeable, 'closed': False}
02126	        with self._lock:
02127	            self._items.append(item)
02128	        return item
02129	
02130	    def take(self, handle):
02131	        """Remove one handle from the table: its owner holds it now."""
02132	        with self._lock:
02133	            for index, item in enumerate(self._items):
02134	                if item['handle'] == handle:
02135	                    del self._items[index]
02136	                    return item
02137	        return None
02138	
02139	    def find(self, handle):
02140	        """The entry of one handle, without removing it from the table.
02141	
02142	        The entry carries the **owner** of the handle and its close
02143	        eligibility.  A close that is confirmed needs the owner of the
02144	        handle in order to notify it: a notification that names a copy
02145	        of the entry would notify nobody.
02146	        """
02147	        with self._lock:
02148	            for item in self._items:
02149	                if item['handle'] == handle:
02150	                    return item
02151	        return None
02152	
02153	    def transfer(self, handle, owner, *, owner_ref=None):
02154	        """Hand one pending handle over to its owner.
02155	
02156	        The owner of the handle is only changed after the handover
02157	        succeeded.  The handle stays in the table until it is closed: it
02158	        is a resource that this round owns and it is named until then.
02159	        A handle that could not be handed over keeps the owner
02160	        ``PENDING`` and it is closed by the round.
02161	
02162	        The ``owner_ref`` is the **object** of the new owner.  It is
02163	        what the pending pass asks before it closes a handle: a read end
02164	        that a live reader still reads from is never closed under it.
02165	        """
02166	        with self._lock:
02167	            for item in self._items:
02168	                if item['handle'] == handle:
02169	                    item['owner'] = owner
02170	                    if owner_ref is not None:
02171	                        item['owner_ref'] = owner_ref
02172	                    return item
02173	        return None
02174	
02175	    def pending(self):
02176	        """The handles that this round still owns and has not closed."""
02177	        with self._lock:
02178	            return [dict(item) for item in self._items]
02179	
02180	    def pending_facts(self):
02181	        """The pending handles of the round as **serialisable** facts.
02182	
02183	        The close eligibility of a handle is a **callable** over the
02184	        owner of the handle and a callable is never serialised.  The
02185	        facts name the eligibility as a name and they keep the
02186	        **original** reference of the handle: a resource of the round is
02187	        never replaced by a copy of a message.
02188	        """
02189	        facts = []
02190	        for item in self.pending():
02191	            predicate = item.get('closeable')
02192	            if predicate is None:
02193	                eligibility = 'NONE'
02194	            elif callable(predicate):
02195	                eligibility = 'CALLABLE'
02196	            else:
02197	                eligibility = 'FROZEN'
02198	            facts.append({'handle': _json_fact(item.get('handle')),
02199	                          'kind': item.get('kind'),
02200	                          'role': item.get('role'),
02201	                          'owner': item.get('owner'),
02202	                          'closed': bool(item.get('closed')),
02203	                          'eligibility': eligibility})
02204	        return facts
02205	
02206	    def close_all(self, failures, on_closed=None, protect=None):
02207	        """Close every handle that is still pending and name the failures.
02208	
02209	        A handle that could not be closed stays in the table: it is a
02210	        resource that this round still owns and it is named in the
02211	        evidence of the round.  It is never left to the garbage
02212	        collector and it is never closed twice.
02213	
02214	        Every handle is closed on its own: a cancellation in the middle
02215	        of the group never skips the handles that follow it.  The
02216	        cancellation is raised after every handle was coordinated and it
02217	        is never turned into an ordinary evidence.
02218	
02219	        A handle that was closed with a **confirmed** close is reported
02220	        to the owner of the table through ``on_closed``: the core has to
02221	        learn that the handle is really closed, so that it never names a
02222	        closed handle any more.  The report happens before the
02223	        cancellation is raised: the bookkeeping of the core is never
02224	        left behind the error that left the cleanup.
02225	
02226	        The ``protect`` predicate is the **real** guard of the pass: a
02227	        handle whose owner is still using it is never closed under it,
02228	        whatever the branch that reached the pass did before.  The
02229	        guard is asked about the owner and not about a name of an owner.
02230	
02231	        The **close eligibility** of a handle is asked here as well: a
02232	        handle that may not be closed yet - the control handle of a
02233	        process whose exit was not confirmed - is never closed by the
02234	        pass.  The eligibility comes from the **same** owner that the
02235	        release of the records asks: the pass may not decide the
02236	        question on its own and it may not close behind the back of the
02237	        owner of the handle.
02238	        """
02239	        remaining = []
02240	        cancellations = []
02241	        with self._lock:
02242	            items = list(self._items)
02243	        for item in items:
02244	            if protect is not None and protect(item):
02245	                # The handle is in use by a live owner: it is never
02246	                # closed under it and it keeps its owner.
02247	                remaining.append(item)
02248	                failures.append({'stage': 'close_pending', 'kind': item['kind'],
02249	                                 'role': item['role'],
02250	                                 'code': 'BROKER_PENDING_HANDLE_IN_USE'})
02251	                continue
02252	            if not self._is_closeable(item):
02253	                # The handle may not be closed yet: it keeps its owner
02254	                # and the round keeps the fact.
02255	                remaining.append(item)
02256	                failures.append({'stage': 'close_pending', 'kind': item['kind'],
02257	                                 'role': item['role'],
02258	                                 'code': 'BROKER_PENDING_CLOSE_NOT_ELIGIBLE'})
02259	                continue
02260	            try:
02261	                self._win32.close_handle(item['handle'])
02262	            except BrokerError as error:
02263	                failures.append({'stage': 'close_pending', 'kind': item['kind'],
02264	                                 'role': item['role'], 'code': error.code})
02265	                remaining.append(item)
02266	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
02267	                failures.append({'stage': 'close_pending', 'kind': item['kind'],
02268	                                 'role': item['role'],
02269	                                 'error_type': type(error).__name__,
02270	                                 'cancellation': True})
02271	                remaining.append(item)
02272	                cancellations.append(error)
02273	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02274	                failures.append({'stage': 'close_pending', 'kind': item['kind'],
02275	                                 'role': item['role'],
02276	                                 'error_type': type(error).__name__})
02277	                remaining.append(item)
02278	            else:
02279	                item['closed'] = True
02280	                if on_closed is not None:
02281	                    on_closed(item)
02282	        with self._lock:
02283	            self._items = remaining
02284	        if cancellations:
02285	            raise cancellations[0]
02286	        return remaining
02287	
02288	    @staticmethod
02289	    def _is_closeable(item):
02290	        """Whether one pending handle may be closed now.
02291	
02292	        The predicate of the entry is the authority of the question.  A
02293	        predicate that cannot answer protects the handle: an unknown
02294	        fact is never a permission to close.
02295	
02296	        The predicate is a **callable** over the original owner of the
02297	        handle.  A frozen answer - a boolean that was taken at the
02298	        moment of the creation of the handle - is **not** an
02299	        eligibility: it protects the handle, because the current fact
02300	        of the owner is the only authority of the question.
02301	        """
02302	        predicate = item.get('closeable')
02303	        if predicate is None:
02304	            return True
02305	        if not callable(predicate):
02306	            # A frozen answer is not an eligibility.
02307	            return False
02308	        try:
02309	            return bool(predicate())
02310	        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02311	            return False
02312	
02313	
02314	# -- the core of the broker ------------------------------------------------
02315	
02316	class BrokerCore:
02317	    """The job and the handle custody of one native custody round.
02318	
02319	    The core owns exactly one job object and the original handles of the
02320	    processes of the round.  Every process of the round is created
02321	    suspended, it is assigned to the job of the round and only then it is
02322	    resumed.
02323	
02324	    A failure never falls back to a weaker behaviour.  There is no retry
02325	    without the job, there is no adoption of a process that was not
02326	    created here and there is no stop of a process that this core does not
02327	    hold a handle of.
02328	    """
02329	
02330	    def __init__(self, *, win32, plan, run_root, clock=None, wall=None,
02331	                 context=None):
02332	        self.win32 = win32
02333	        self.plan = plan
02334	        self.run_root = Path(run_root)
02335	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
02336	        self._wall = wall or time.time
02337	        # The ONE context of the round.  The core, the loop, the
02338	        # transport of the channel and the endpoints of the channel hold
02339	        # this very object: a layer that only copied the number of a
02340	        # deadline would be a layer with a deadline of its own.
02341	        self.context = context if context is not None else _RoundContext(
02342	            clock=self._clock, wall=self._wall)
02343	        # The façade of the primitives reads the absolute deadline and
02344	        # the published cleanup deadline of the round from the **same**
02345	        # context: a recovery of the façade may not open a budget of
02346	        # its own.
02347	        binder = getattr(self.win32, 'bind_context', None)
02348	        if callable(binder):
02349	            try:
02350	                binder(self.context)
02351	            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02352	                pass
02353	        self.state = 'NEW'
02354	        self.job = None
02355	        self.job_kill_on_close = False
02356	        self.job_closed = False
02357	        self.job_terminated = False
02358	        self.job_active = None
02359	        self.job_active_known = False
02360	        self.job_process_ids = []
02361	        self.records = []
02362	        self.failures = []
02363	        self.readers = []
02364	        self.reader_states = []
02365	        self.new_text_bytes = 0
02366	        self.stop_reason = None
02367	        self._stream_failure_reported = False
02368	        self._text_failure_reported = False
02369	        self._pending = _PendingResources(win32)
02370	        # The close eligibility of the control handle of every record of
02371	        # the round.  The map is keyed by the identity of the record and
02372	        # it holds the **callable** over that record: the pending table
02373	        # and the release of the records ask the very same callable and
02374	        # they never disagree about the current exit fact.
02375	        self._process_eligibility = {}
02376	        # The resources that this core could not release.  A resource
02377	        # that could not be closed keeps its original reference and its
02378	        # owner here: it is named in the evidence of the round and it is
02379	        # never replaced by a copy of an error message.
02380	        self._retained_resources = []
02381	        # The cancellations that were observed during the coordination of
02382	        # the readers.  They are raised after every reader was
02383	        # coordinated and they never replace an ordinary failure.
02384	        self._readers_cancelled = []
02385	        # The cancellations that were observed during the wait of the
02386	        # original processes.  They are raised after every process was
02387	        # coordinated.
02388	        self._processes_cancelled = []
02389	        # The absolute deadline of the round.  It is bound by the loop
02390	        # before the job of the round exists and it is the upper bound of
02391	        # the one cleanup deadline of the round.
02392	        self._absolute_deadline = None
02393	        # The ONE cleanup deadline of the round.  It is generated at the
02394	        # first stop or at the first failure of the cleanup and it is the
02395	        # same value in every step of the cleanup.
02396	        self._cleanup_deadline_value = None
02397	        self._released = False
02398	        self._abort_reported = False
02399	        self.broker_identity = {
02400	            'pid': os.getpid(),
02401	            'executable': sys.executable,
02402	            'reported_by_the_broker': True,
02403	            'identity_source': 'CHILD_CLAIM',
02404	        }
02405	
02406	    # -- the deadlines of the round ---------------------------------------
02407	    def bind_clock(self, clock):
02408	        """Bind the clock of the round to this core.
02409	
02410	        The core, the loop and the context of the round read the same
02411	        clock: the absolute deadline of the round and the ONE cleanup
02412	        deadline are in one domain and a deadline of one domain is never
02413	        compared with a clock of another one.
02414	        """
02415	        if clock is not None:
02416	            self._clock = clock
02417	            self.context.clock = clock
02418	        return self._clock
02419	
02420	    def bind_absolute_deadline(self, deadline):
02421	        """Bind the absolute deadline of the round to this core.
02422	
02423	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
02424	        bound.  It is the upper bound of the one cleanup deadline of the
02425	        round: the cleanup never outlives the round itself.
02426	        """
02427	        value = self.context.bind_absolute_deadline(deadline)
02428	        self._absolute_deadline = value
02429	        # The façade of the primitives is bound to the same deadline: its
02430	        # waits are bounded by the deadline of the round and they never
02431	        # start a wait with a fresh timeout of their own.
02432	        binder = getattr(self.win32, 'bind_deadline', None)
02433	        if callable(binder):
02434	            try:
02435	                binder(value, self._clock)
02436	            except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02437	                pass
02438	        return value
02439	
02440	    def cleanup_deadline(self, deadline=None):
02441	        """The ONE absolute deadline of the cleanup of this round.
02442	
02443	        The deadline is generated once, at the first stop or at the first
02444	        failure of the cleanup, and it is published **atomically** by the
02445	        context of the round.  Two callers that entered the cleanup at
02446	        the same moment therefore see the very same value and the
02447	        deadline is never generated twice.
02448	
02449	        It is the earlier of the absolute deadline of the round and the
02450	        fixed cleanup budget, measured from the moment of the first
02451	        cleanup.  Every later call returns the very same value: no layer
02452	        of the cleanup adds a budget of its own and no layer starts a
02453	        new wait with a fresh timeout.
02454	        """
02455	        value = self.context.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
02456	        self._cleanup_deadline_value = value
02457	        return value
02458	
02459	    def remaining_cleanup_seconds(self, deadline=None):
02460	        """The seconds that are left until the cleanup deadline.
02461	
02462	        The value is recomputed on every call: a wait that already
02463	        consumed part of the budget never gets the whole budget again.
02464	        """
02465	        return self.context.remaining_cleanup(deadline)
02466	
02467	    def check_startup_budget(self, stage):
02468	        """Check the shared budget of the startup **before** a step.
02469	
02470	        A step of the startup of the round - the creation of a process,
02471	        the assignment of a process to the job, the resume of its
02472	        thread, the start of a reader and the creation of the next role
02473	        - checks the shared cancel and the shared deadline of the round
02474	        before it happens and after it happened.
02475	
02476	        A step that runs after the deadline arrived may only clean up:
02477	        it never resumes a thread and it never creates the next role.
02478	        """
02479	        context = self.context
02480	        if context.cancel.is_set() or context.is_unknown():
02481	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STARTUP_CANCELLED', stage)
02482	        deadline = context.absolute_deadline
02483	        if deadline is not None and self._clock() >= deadline:
02484	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STARTUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED', stage)
02485	        return True
02486	
02487	    def _retain_resource(self, kind, handle, code=None, owner='CORE_RETAINED'):
02488	        """Keep the original reference of a resource that was not released."""
02489	        for item in self._retained_resources:
02490	            if item['kind'] == kind and item['handle'] is handle:
02491	                return item
02492	        item = {'kind': kind, 'handle': handle, 'owner': owner, 'code': code}
02493	        self._retained_resources.append(item)
02494	        return item
02495	
02496	    def _release_resource(self, kind, handle):
02497	        """Forget a resource that was released with a confirmed close.
02498	
02499	        The **original reference** of a resource is its identity: a
02500	        handle that was really closed is forgotten whatever name it was
02501	        retained under.  A resource that was not closed keeps its owner
02502	        and it stays named: the retained list is never cleared to make a
02503	        check green.
02504	        """
02505	        self._retained_resources = [item for item in self._retained_resources
02506	                                   if item['handle'] is not handle]
02507	
02508	    def _adopt_from_api(self, entries, role):
02509	        """Take the named handles over from the façade of the primitives.
02510	
02511	        The receiver registers the handles and the façade forgets them
02512	        in **one** atomic step.  A handle that both of them names would
02513	        have two independent owners and the recovery of the façade could
02514	        close a handle that a live reader of the round is reading from.
02515	        A handle that left the façade before its receiver registered it
02516	        would have no owner at all.
02517	
02518	        The façade that does not offer the atomic handover is asked to
02519	        forget the handles after the registration: the receiver is
02520	        always registered first, so that a failure in between leaves the
02521	        handle with **two** owners and never with none.
02522	        """
02523	        items = list(entries)
02524	
02525	        def register(handles):
02526	            for handle, kind in handles:
02527	                self._pending.add(handle, kind, role=role)
02528	
02529	        handover = getattr(self.win32, 'handover', None)
02530	        if callable(handover):
02531	            handover(items, owner='CORE', register=register)
02532	            return
02533	        register(items)
02534	        forget = getattr(self.win32, 'forget_handle', None)
02535	        if callable(forget):
02536	            for handle, _kind in items:
02537	                forget(handle)
02538	
02539	    def _api_retained(self):
02540	        """The original handles that the Windows façade still holds.
02541	
02542	        The façade is the owner of a handle that it could not hand over
02543	        and it is the owner of the recovery of that handle.  The core
02544	        keeps the façade, so the core can always reach these handles.
02545	
02546	        The names of the handles are the printable ones: the evidence of
02547	        a round is a document and it never carries a raw handle value.
02548	        """
02549	        getter = getattr(self.win32, 'retained_handles', None)
02550	        if not callable(getter):
02551	            return []
02552	        try:
02553	            entries = list(getter())
02554	        except BaseException:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02555	            return []
02556	        return [{'kind': entry.get('kind'), 'pid': entry.get('pid'),
02557	                 'stage': entry.get('stage'), 'owner': entry.get('owner'),
02558	                 'failures': list(entry.get('failures', ())),
02559	                 'handle_repr': repr(entry.get('handle'))}
02560	                for entry in entries]
02561	
02562	    # -- the arming of the round ------------------------------------------
02563	    def arm(self):
02564	        """Create the job object of the round.
02565	
02566	        The job is created before any process of the round exists, so that
02567	        every process of the round can be assigned to it before it runs.
02568	
02569	        The shared budget of the startup is checked **before** the
02570	        creation of the job and **after** it: a job that was created
02571	        after the deadline of the round arrived is only cleaned up and it
02572	        is never armed.
02573	        """
02574	        if self.state != 'NEW':
02575	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STATE_INVALID', self.state)
02576	        self.state = 'VALIDATED'
02577	        self.check_startup_budget('ARM:JOB')
02578	        try:
02579	            self.job = self.win32.create_job()
02580	        except BrokerError as error:
02581	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_job', 'code': error.code})
02582	            self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
02583	            raise
02584	        self._pending.add(self.job, 'job')
02585	        self._pending.transfer(self.job, 'CORE')
02586	        try:
02587	            self.win32.set_job_kill_on_close(self.job)
02588	        except BrokerError as error:
02589	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'set_job_limits', 'code': error.code})
02590	            closed = self._close_handle('job', self.job)
02591	            if closed:
02592	                # The job handle was closed with a confirmed close: it may
02593	                # be forgotten.
02594	                self.job = None
02595	            else:
02596	                # The job handle was not closed.  The original reference
02597	                # stays with its owner and it is named: a handle that may
02598	                # not be closed is never forgotten and it is never
02599	                # replaced by a copy of an error message.
02600	                self._retain_resource('job', self.job, error.code)
02601	            self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
02602	            raise
02603	        self.job_kill_on_close = True
02604	        self.state = 'ARMED'
02605	        # The budget is checked after the creation of the job: a job that
02606	        # was created after the deadline arrived is only cleaned up.
02607	        self.check_startup_budget('ARM:DONE')
02608	        return self.evidence()
02609	
02610	    # -- the launch of the roles ------------------------------------------
02611	    def launch(self):
02612	        """Create the roles of the round, one after the other.
02613	
02614	        The launch has an independent ``finally``: a failure of a role,
02615	        a refusal of the broker and a cancellation all release the
02616	        resources of the round before the failure leaves this method.
02617	        The cancellation itself is not swallowed: it continues to
02618	        propagate after the cleanup.
02619	        """
02620	        if self.state != 'ARMED':
02621	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_STATE_INVALID', self.state)
02622	        try:
02623	            for role in self.plan.roles:
02624	                # The shared budget of the startup is checked before
02625	                # every role: a role that is created after the deadline
02626	                # of the round arrived is a role that nobody may stop.
02627	                self.check_startup_budget('ROLE:' + role['role'])
02628	                self._launch_role(role)
02629	        except BaseException:
02630	            # Any failure of the launch, a cancellation included, ends
02631	            # the round closed: the job, the pipes and the original
02632	            # handles of this round are released here.
02633	            self._abort_round('LAUNCH_ABORTED')
02634	            raise
02635	        self.state = 'RUNNING'
02636	        return self.evidence()
02637	
02638	    def _launch_role(self, role):
02639	        name = role['role']
02640	        owned = [record['pid'] for record in self.records if record['pid']]
02641	        command = role_command(role, self.run_root, owned)
02642	        record = {'role': name,
02643	                  'executable': command['executable'],
02644	                  'executable_sha256': role['tools'][0]['sha256'],
02645	                  'arguments': list(command['arguments']),
02646	                  'working_directory': command['working_directory'],
02647	                  'log': role['log'],
02648	                  'created': False, 'assigned': False, 'resumed': False,
02649	                  'pid': None, 'creation_time': None,
02650	                  'terminated': False, 'exited': False, 'exit_code': None,
02651	                  'process_handle_closed': False, 'thread_handle_closed': False,
02652	                  'stdout_bytes': 0, 'stderr_bytes': 0,
02653	                  'stdout_overflow': False, 'stderr_overflow': False,
02654	                  'failure': None,
02655	                  'pending_handles': []}
02656	        self.records.append(record)
02657	        try:
02658	            self._prepare_directories(role)
02659	        except OSError as error:
02660	            record['failure'] = 'BROKER_ROLE_DIRECTORY_FAILED'
02661	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'role_directory', 'role': name,
02662	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
02663	            self._abort_round('ROLE_DIRECTORY_FAILED')
02664	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_ROLE_DIRECTORY_FAILED', name)
02665	        try:
02666	            # The shared budget of the startup is checked **before** every
02667	            # pipe: a pipe that is created after the deadline of the round
02668	            # arrived is a pipe that nobody may close.
02669	            self.check_startup_budget('PIPE:stdout:' + name)
02670	            stdout_read, stdout_write = self.win32.create_pipe('stdout')
02671	            # The two ends are handed over to the core in one step: the
02672	            # façade forgets them and the core registers them, so that
02673	            # the two tables never name the same handle.
02674	            self._adopt_from_api([(stdout_read, 'pipe_read'),
02675	                                  (stdout_write, 'pipe_write')], name)
02676	            self.check_startup_budget('PIPE:stderr:' + name)
02677	            stderr_read, stderr_write = self.win32.create_pipe('stderr')
02678	            self._adopt_from_api([(stderr_read, 'pipe_read'),
02679	                                  (stderr_write, 'pipe_write')], name)
02680	            self.check_startup_budget('PIPE_DONE:' + name)
02681	        except BrokerError as error:
02682	            record['failure'] = error.code
02683	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_pipe', 'role': name, 'code': error.code})
02684	            self._abort_round('PIPE_FAILED')
02685	            raise
02686	        try:
02687	            # The shared budget of the startup is checked **before** the
02688	            # creation: a role that is created after the deadline of the
02689	            # round arrived would be a role that nobody may stop.
02690	            self.check_startup_budget('CREATE:' + name)
02691	            created = self.win32.create_process_suspended(
02692	                executable=command['executable'], arguments=command['arguments'],
02693	                working_directory=command['working_directory'],
02694	                stdout_handle=stdout_write, stderr_handle=stderr_write,
02695	                environment=command['environment'])
02696	        except BrokerError as error:
02697	            record['failure'] = error.code
02698	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_process', 'role': name,
02699	                                  'code': error.code})
02700	            # The façade keeps the original handles of a process that it
02701	            # could not hand over.  They are named here: the core holds
02702	            # the façade and the recovery of the façade can reach them.
02703	            retained = self._api_retained()
02704	            if retained:
02705	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_process_retained',
02706	                                      'role': name, 'retained': retained})
02707	            self._abort_round('CREATE_FAILED')
02708	            raise
02709	        # The original handles of the process enter the pending table of
02710	        # the round in the moment of their creation and the core takes
02711	        # them right away: it is their owner from now on and it closes
02712	        # them when it releases the records.
02713	        #
02714	        # The **eligibility** of the control handle is a callable over
02715	        # the ORIGINAL record: the pending pass asks it at the moment
02716	        # of its close and it reads the current exit fact of the
02717	        # record.  The release of the records asks the very same
02718	        # callable, so that the record and the pending table never
02719	        # disagree about the question "may this handle be closed now?".
02720	        #
02721	        # The registration happens **before** the check of the shared
02722	        # budget that follows the creation and before every other step
02723	        # that may raise.  A process that was created after the deadline
02724	        # arrived is only cleaned up and it is never owned by nobody:
02725	        # the lease of the round may end, the ownership of the handles
02726	        # may not.
02727	        eligibility = self._process_closeable(record)
02728	        self._process_eligibility[id(record)] = eligibility
02729	        self._pending.add(created.process, 'process', role=name,
02730	                          closeable=eligibility)
02731	        self._pending.add(created.thread, 'thread', role=name)
02732	        self._pending.transfer(created.process, 'CORE')
02733	        self._pending.transfer(created.thread, 'CORE')
02734	        record['pending_handles'].append({'kind': 'process', 'owner': 'CORE'})
02735	        record['pending_handles'].append({'kind': 'thread', 'owner': 'CORE'})
02736	        record['created'] = True
02737	        record['pid'] = created.pid
02738	        record['creation_time'] = created.creation_time
02739	        record['process_handle'] = created.process
02740	        record['thread_handle'] = created.thread
02741	        for failure in getattr(created, 'failures', ()):
02742	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_process', 'role': name,
02743	                                  'code': failure})
02744	        try:
02745	            # The budget is checked again after the creation: a process
02746	            # that was created after the deadline arrived is only
02747	            # cleaned up, it is never assigned and it is never resumed.
02748	            self.check_startup_budget('CREATED:' + name)
02749	        except BrokerError as error:
02750	            record['failure'] = error.code
02751	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_process', 'role': name,
02752	                                  'code': error.code})
02753	            # The façade keeps the original handles of a process that it
02754	            # could not hand over.  They are named here: the core holds
02755	            # the façade and the recovery of the façade can reach them.
02756	            retained = self._api_retained()
02757	            if retained:
02758	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'create_process_retained',
02759	                                      'role': name, 'retained': retained})
02760	            self._abort_round('CREATE_FAILED')
02761	            raise
02762	        # The write ends belong to the child now; the broker keeps the read
02763	        # ends only.  The copies of the broker are closed and they leave
02764	        # the pending table: they are not pending any more.
02765	        self._release_pending(stdout_write)
02766	        self._release_pending(stderr_write)
02767	        try:
02768	            # The shared budget is checked before the assignment and
02769	            # before the resume: a thread that is resumed after the
02770	            # deadline of the round arrived is a service that runs
02771	            # outside of the round.
02772	            self.check_startup_budget('ASSIGN:' + name)
02773	            self.win32.assign_process_to_job(self.job, created.process)
02774	        except BrokerError as error:
02775	            record['failure'] = error.code
02776	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'assign', 'role': name, 'code': error.code})
02777	            # The process is still suspended: it never ran.  It is stopped
02778	            # through its own original handle and its exit is confirmed.
02779	            self._terminate_owned(created.process, record, 'ASSIGN_FAILED')
02780	            self._abort_round('ASSIGN_FAILED')
02781	            raise
02782	        record['assigned'] = True
02783	        try:
02784	            self.check_startup_budget('RESUME:' + name)
02785	            self.win32.resume_thread(created.thread)
02786	        except BrokerError as error:
02787	            record['failure'] = error.code
02788	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'resume', 'role': name, 'code': error.code})
02789	            self._abort_round('RESUME_FAILED')
02790	            raise
02791	        record['resumed'] = True
02792	        try:
02793	            self.check_startup_budget('READER:' + name)
02794	            self._start_reader(record, stdout_read, 'stdout')
02795	            self.check_startup_budget('READER:' + name)
02796	            self._start_reader(record, stderr_read, 'stderr')
02797	        except BrokerError as error:
02798	            record['failure'] = error.code
02799	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'reader', 'role': name, 'code': error.code})
02800	            self._abort_round('READER_FAILED')
02801	            raise
02802	
02803	    def _release_pending(self, handle):
02804	        """Release one pending handle that the child took over."""
02805	        item = self._pending.take(handle)
02806	        if item is None:
02807	            return False
02808	        try:
02809	            self.win32.close_handle(handle)
02810	        except BrokerError as error:
02811	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_stream', 'kind': item['kind'],
02812	                                  'role': item['role'], 'code': error.code})
02813	            self._pending.add(handle, item['kind'], role=item['role'])
02814	            return False
02815	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
02816	            # A cancellation never becomes an ordinary evidence: it is
02817	            # named and it is raised, so that the cleanups that follow
02818	            # it still run.
02819	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_stream', 'kind': item['kind'],
02820	                                  'role': item['role'],
02821	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__,
02822	                                  'cancellation': True})
02823	            self._pending.add(handle, item['kind'], role=item['role'])
02824	            raise
02825	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02826	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_stream', 'kind': item['kind'],
02827	                                  'role': item['role'],
02828	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
02829	            self._pending.add(handle, item['kind'], role=item['role'])
02830	            return False
02831	        return True
02832	
02833	    def _prepare_directories(self, role):
02834	        """Create the fresh directories of a role.
02835	
02836	        A directory that already exists is refused: a stale log, a stale
02837	        pid lease or a stale sampler output of an earlier round may never
02838	        be read as the output of this round.
02839	
02840	        The user directory of the ComfyUI role is the directory that the
02841	        plan closed and not a fixed name: the round creates the
02842	        directory that the command of the role really uses.
02843	        """
02844	        name = role['role']
02845	        if name == 'COMFY':
02846	            (Path(self.run_root) / 'temp' / 'service-os').mkdir(parents=True,
02847	                                                                exist_ok=True)
02848	            (Path(self.run_root) / role['data']['user_directory']).mkdir(
02849	                parents=True, exist_ok=True)
02850	        elif name == 'SAMPLER':
02851	            (Path(self.run_root) / 'temp' / 'resource-sampler').mkdir(
02852	                parents=True, exist_ok=False)
02853	
02854	    def _start_reader(self, record, handle, stream):
02855	        """Hand the read end of a pipe over to its reader.
02856	
02857	        The reader is the owner of the read end from now on: the
02858	        handover changes the owner of the handle and nothing else.  The
02859	        handle stays in the pending table of the round until it is
02860	        really closed, and the reader closes it when it is stopped.
02861	
02862	        The reader **object** is registered as the owner of the handle
02863	        and not only its name: the pending pass asks the reader whether
02864	        it is still alive before it closes the read end under it.
02865	        """
02866	        reader = _BoundedStreamReader(self.win32, handle, limit=MAX_STREAM_BYTES,
02867	                                      name=record['role'] + '-' + stream)
02868	        self.readers.append(reader)
02869	        self._pending.transfer(handle, 'READER', owner_ref=reader)
02870	        record['pending_handles'].append({'kind': 'pipe_read',
02871	                                          'stream': stream, 'owner': 'READER'})
02872	        reader.start()
02873	
02874	    @staticmethod
02875	    def _process_closeable(record):
02876	        """The close eligibility of the control handle of a process.
02877	
02878	        The control handle of a process is closed **only** when the exit
02879	        of that process was really confirmed.  The fact comes from the
02880	        **record** of the process and it is not a name of a state: a
02881	        process whose exit was not confirmed may still be alive and its
02882	        control handle is the only handle that may stop it.
02883	
02884	        The eligibility is a **callable** over the original record and
02885	        never a frozen copy of a fact: the pending pass asks it at the
02886	        moment of its close and it reads the **current** exit fact of
02887	        the record.  A pending entry that froze the answer at the
02888	        moment of the creation of the process would never close the
02889	        handle of a process whose exit was confirmed later.
02890	
02891	        The same eligibility is asked by the release of the records and
02892	        by the pending pass of the round: the pending pass may not
02893	        close behind the back of the owner of the handle.
02894	        """
02895	        def eligible():
02896	            return bool(record.get('exited'))
02897	        return eligible
02898	
02899	    @staticmethod
02900	    def _process_exit_confirmed(record):
02901	        """Whether the exit of the process of a record was confirmed.
02902	
02903	        The fact is read from the **original** record and it is asked at
02904	        the moment of the decision: a record whose exit was confirmed
02905	        after the creation of the process is a record whose control
02906	        handle may be closed.
02907	        """
02908	        return bool(record.get('exited'))
02909	
02910	    def _process_eligible(self, record):
02911	        """Whether the control handle of the process of a record may be closed.
02912	
02913	        The **very same** callable that the pending table of the round
02914	        holds is asked: the record and the pending table never disagree
02915	        about the current exit fact of the process.  A record without
02916	        an eligibility of its own is asked directly: the fact comes
02917	        from the record and it is never a name of a state.
02918	        """
02919	        eligibility = self._process_eligibility.get(id(record))
02920	        if eligibility is None:
02921	            return self._process_exit_confirmed(record)
02922	        return bool(eligibility())
02923	
02924	    def _close_handle(self, kind, handle):
02925	        """Close one owned handle and name a failure of the close.
02926	
02927	        A handle that was closed leaves the pending table of the round
02928	        and it leaves the retained resources of the round: a handle that
02929	        is really closed is never named as a resource any more.  A
02930	        handle that could not be closed stays pending: it is a resource
02931	        that this round still owns and it is named.
02932	
02933	        A **confirmed** close is notified through the **one** notification
02934	        of the round: the job of the round, the records of the round, the
02935	        reader that owns the handle and the retained resources of the
02936	        round learn the very same fact, and every owner of a handle that
02937	        was really closed knows that it must never close it again.
02938	
02939	        A **cancellation** in the middle of a close is never turned into
02940	        an ordinary evidence: it is named and it is raised, so that the
02941	        finalizer that follows it still runs and the cancellation
02942	        continues to propagate after the cleanup.
02943	        """
02944	        entry = self._pending.find(handle)
02945	        try:
02946	            self.win32.close_handle(handle)
02947	        except BrokerError as error:
02948	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
02949	                                  'code': error.code})
02950	            return False
02951	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
02952	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
02953	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__,
02954	                                  'cancellation': True})
02955	            raise
02956	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02957	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
02958	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
02959	            return False
02960	        self._pending.take(handle)
02961	        # The handle is really closed: every owner of it is notified
02962	        # through the one notification of the round.
02963	        self._note_closed_handle(entry if entry is not None
02964	                                 else {'handle': handle, 'kind': kind})
02965	        return True
02966	
02967	    def _close_readers(self):
02968	        """Stop the readers of the children and close their read handles.
02969	
02970	        The wait of a reader is bounded by the ONE cleanup deadline of the
02971	        round and its state is recorded.  A reader that is still blocked
02972	        when the wait is over is a *fact* of this round and it is
02973	        reported as such: a daemon thread that is still running is not a
02974	        finished thread, and the read end of such a reader is not closed
02975	        under it.
02976	
02977	        Every reader is independent of the others: a reader that could
02978	        not be joined never skips the coordination of the readers that
02979	        follow it, and a reader whose thread never started is never
02980	        joined at all.  A read end that was closed with a confirmed
02981	        close is never closed a second time.
02982	        """
02983	        for reader in self.readers:
02984	            try:
02985	                self._close_one_reader(reader)
02986	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
02987	                # A cancellation never skips the readers that follow it:
02988	                # it is named and it is raised after every reader was
02989	                # coordinated.
02990	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_readers',
02991	                                      'name': reader.name,
02992	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
02993	                                      'cancellation': True})
02994	                self._readers_cancelled.append(error)
02995	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
02996	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_readers',
02997	                                      'name': reader.name,
02998	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
02999	        if self._readers_cancelled:
03000	            raise self._readers_cancelled[0]
03001	
03002	    def _close_one_reader(self, reader):
03003	        """Coordinate one reader of the round on its own.
03004	
03005	        The read end of a reader is closed **only** when the reader
03006	        really stopped.  Every branch of the coordination asks the
03007	        reader whether it is still alive: a wait that was cancelled and
03008	        a join that failed are not a proof that the reader ended, and
03009	        the read end of a live reader is never closed under it.
03010	
03011	        A cancellation in the middle of the coordination never skips the
03012	        readers that follow it and it never skips the protection of the
03013	        read end of the reader that is still alive.
03014	        """
03015	        if reader.handle is None:
03016	            # The read end of this reader was closed with a confirmed
03017	            # close already.
03018	            return
03019	        state = {'name': reader.name,
03020	                 'finished': reader.finished.is_set(),
03021	                 'joined': False,
03022	                 'alive': reader.is_alive(),
03023	                 'error': reader.error}
03024	        if reader.ident is None and not reader.is_alive():
03025	            # The thread of this reader never started.  A thread that
03026	            # never started is never joined: the join of a thread that
03027	            # was not started is an error of the caller and not a fact
03028	            # of the round.  The handle of this reader is closed here,
03029	            # because no thread of the round reads it.
03030	            state['started'] = False
03031	            state['alive'] = False
03032	            state['finished'] = True
03033	            self.reader_states.append(state)
03034	            if self._close_handle('stream', reader.handle):
03035	                reader.handle = None
03036	            else:
03037	                self._retain_resource('pipe_read', reader.handle,
03038	                                      'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
03039	            return
03040	        state['started'] = True
03041	        cancelled = None
03042	        try:
03043	            remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
03044	            wait = min(READER_JOIN_SECONDS, remaining)
03045	            if wait > 0:
03046	                reader.finished.wait(wait)
03047	            # The remaining budget is recomputed before the join: a wait
03048	            # that already consumed part of the budget never receives the
03049	            # whole budget again.
03050	            remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
03051	            reader.join(min(READER_JOIN_SECONDS, remaining))
03052	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
03053	            # A cancellation never decides the fate of the read end: the
03054	            # reader is asked again whether it is still alive.
03055	            cancelled = error
03056	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03057	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_reader',
03058	                                  'name': reader.name,
03059	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03060	        alive = reader.is_alive()
03061	        state['finished'] = reader.finished.is_set()
03062	        state['joined'] = not alive
03063	        state['alive'] = alive
03064	        self.reader_states.append(state)
03065	        if alive:
03066	            # The reader still holds the read end.  The handle is not
03067	            # closed under a running reader: it leaves the pending
03068	            # table with its owner and the round keeps the fact.
03069	            self._pending.take(reader.handle)
03070	            self.reader_states[-1]['handle_retained'] = True
03071	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'reader_not_stopped',
03072	                                  'name': reader.name,
03073	                                  'code': 'BROKER_READER_NOT_STOPPED'})
03074	            if cancelled is not None:
03075	                raise cancelled
03076	            return
03077	        if self._close_handle('stream', reader.handle):
03078	            reader.handle = None
03079	        else:
03080	            self._retain_resource('pipe_read', reader.handle,
03081	                                  'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
03082	        if cancelled is not None:
03083	            raise cancelled
03084	
03085	    def _terminate_owned(self, handle, record, reason):
03086	        """Stop one owned process through its own original handle.
03087	
03088	        No pid is used and no name is used: only the handle that this core
03089	        created is passed to the operating system.  The wait is bounded
03090	        by the ONE cleanup deadline of the round: an expired deadline
03091	        never starts a new wait.
03092	
03093	        The termination and the **confirmation of the exit** are two
