03094	        independent facts.  A termination that failed is named and it
03095	        is preserved, and the **bounded wait** of the exit still runs
03096	        when the original deadline of the round still permits it: a
03097	        process whose termination failed may have exited anyway, and
03098	        the exit is the only fact that may release its control handle.
03099	        A failure of the termination never skips the wait and it never
03100	        replaces it.
03101	
03102	        The control handle of a process is never closed because the
03103	        termination was **requested**: only a confirmed exit may close
03104	        it, and an expired cleanup keeps the control and it never opens
03105	        a new budget of its own.
03106	        """
03107	        terminated = False
03108	        try:
03109	            self.win32.terminate_process(handle, 1)
03110	            record['terminated'] = True
03111	            terminated = True
03112	        except BrokerError as error:
03113	            # The original failure of the round is named and it stays
03114	            # named: the wait of the exit below is independent of it.
03115	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate', 'reason': reason,
03116	                                  'code': error.code})
03117	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
03118	            # A cancellation is never swallowed: it leaves the method
03119	            # and the finalizers that follow it still run.
03120	            raise
03121	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03122	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate', 'reason': reason,
03123	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03124	        timeout = self._wait_timeout_ms()
03125	        if timeout <= 0:
03126	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'reason': reason,
03127	                                  'code': 'BROKER_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'})
03128	            return terminated
03129	        try:
03130	            record['exited'] = bool(self.win32.wait_process(handle, timeout))
03131	        except BrokerError as error:
03132	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'reason': reason, 'code': error.code})
03133	        if record['exited']:
03134	            try:
03135	                record['exit_code'] = self.win32.exit_code(handle)
03136	            except BrokerError as error:
03137	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'reason': reason,
03138	                                      'code': error.code})
03139	        return terminated
03140	
03141	    # -- the stop of the round ---------------------------------------------
03142	    def stop(self, reason='STOP', *, force_unknown=False, deadline=None):
03143	        """Stop the round and release every handle that this core holds.
03144	
03145	        The stop is bounded and it is idempotent: a repeated stop does not
03146	        terminate anything twice and it does not change the evidence of
03147	        the round.  Every handle is reported on its own, so a handle that
03148	        could not be closed stays visible and the round becomes unknown
03149	        instead of a silent success.
03150	
03151	        The steps of the stop are **independent bounded finalizers**.  The
03152	        termination of the job, the wait for the original processes, the
03153	        stop of the readers, the query of the job, the release of the
03154	        original handles, the release of the job, the release of the
03155	        pending resources and the diagnosis of the round each run in
03156	        their own guard: a failure of one of them never skips the others.
03157	        The first failure of the round is the one that leaves the method
03158	        and the later failures are named next to it.
03159	
03160	        A stop that did not release everything does **not** latch the
03161	        round as released: the same owner may try the remaining work
03162	        again, inside the ONE cleanup deadline of the round.  A handle
03163	        that was closed with a confirmed close is never closed a second
03164	        time.
03165	        """
03166	        if self._released:
03167	            return self.evidence()
03168	        if self.state == 'STOPPING' and self._cleanup_deadline_value is not None:
03169	            # A stop that is already running: the owner of the round does
03170	            # not start a second one.
03171	            return self.evidence()
03172	        self.state = 'STOPPING'
03173	        if self.stop_reason is None:
03174	            self.stop_reason = reason
03175	        # The ONE cleanup deadline of the round.  It is generated here,
03176	        # at the first stop, and every step below uses this very value.
03177	        deadline = self.cleanup_deadline(deadline)
03178	        primary = None
03179	
03180	        def step(stage, action):
03181	            """Run one bounded finalizer of the stop.
03182	
03183	            A finalizer that fails never skips the finalizers that follow
03184	            it.  A cancellation is named as a cancellation and it is
03185	            never turned into an ordinary evidence: it becomes the
03186	            primary failure of the round when nothing else failed, and
03187	            it is raised after every finalizer was executed.
03188	            """
03189	            nonlocal primary
03190	            try:
03191	                action()
03192	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the cleanup still runs
03193	                cancelled = isinstance(error, (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit))
03194	                if primary is None:
03195	                    primary = error
03196	                self.failures.append({'stage': stage,
03197	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
03198	                                      'cancellation': cancelled})
03199	
03200	        step('diagnostic', self._collect_stream_counts)
03201	        step('terminate_job', self._terminate_the_job)
03202	        step('wait_processes', self._wait_the_processes)
03203	        step('close_readers', self._close_readers)
03204	        step('query_job', self._query_job)
03205	        step('release_records', self._release_the_records)
03206	        step('release_job', self._release_the_job)
03207	        step('pending_resources', self._close_pending)
03208	
03209	        released = self._is_released()
03210	        # The latch of a completed release is only set when the release
03211	        # was really proven.  An unfinished cleanup never blocks the
03212	        # recovery of the same owner.
03213	        self._released = released
03214	        self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED' if (force_unknown or not released) else 'RELEASED'
03215	        if primary is not None:
03216	            # The first failure of the round is not swallowed: the caller
03217	            # sees it after the round was cleaned up.
03218	            raise primary
03219	        return self.evidence()
03220	
03221	    def _pending_handle_in_use(self, item):
03222	        """Whether the owner of a pending handle is still using it.
03223	
03224	        The owner of a handle is an **object** and not a name: the
03225	        reader that owns a read end is asked whether it is still alive.
03226	        A read end that a live reader still reads from is never closed
03227	        under it, whatever the branch that reached the pending pass did
03228	        before.
03229	        """
03230	        owner = item.get('owner_ref')
03231	        if owner is None:
03232	            return False
03233	        alive = getattr(owner, 'is_alive', None)
03234	        if not callable(alive):
03235	            return False
03236	        try:
03237	            return bool(alive())
03238	        except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03239	            # A reader that cannot answer is a reader that may still be
03240	            # alive: the handle is protected.
03241	            return True
03242	
03243	    def _close_pending(self):
03244	        """Close the handles that are still pending and name the failures.
03245	
03246	        The pending pass is the **last** close of the round and it may
03247	        close a handle that the release of the records or the release of
03248	        the job could not close.  Every handle that it really closes is
03249	        reported to the core: the job of the round, the records of the
03250	        round and the retained resources of the round never name a
03251	        handle that is closed.
03252	
03253	        The pass never closes a handle whose owner is still using it:
03254	        the guard is the fact of the owner and not the name of an
03255	        owner.
03256	        """
03257	        retained = self._pending.close_all(self.failures,
03258	                                           on_closed=self._note_closed_handle,
03259	                                           protect=self._pending_handle_in_use)
03260	        if retained:
03261	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'pending_resources',
03262	                                  'count': len(retained),
03263	                                  'code': 'BROKER_PENDING_RESOURCES_RETAINED'})
03264	
03265	    def _note_closed_handle(self, item):
03266	        """Learn that one handle was really closed.
03267	
03268	        The bookkeeping of the core has to agree with the fact of the
03269	        close: a handle that is closed may never be named by the job of
03270	        the round, by the records of the round or by the retained
03271	        resources of the round.  Only the handle that was really closed
03272	        is forgotten: a handle that could not be closed keeps its owner
03273	        and it stays named.
03274	
03275	        The **owner object** of the handle is notified as well.  A
03276	        reader whose read end was closed by the pending pass learns that
03277	        the handle is gone: a later stop never closes the same original
03278	        handle a second time and it never closes a handle that the
03279	        operating system may have reused in the meantime.
03280	        """
03281	        handle = item['handle']
03282	        kind = item.get('kind')
03283	        if self.job is handle:
03284	            self.job = None
03285	            self.job_closed = True
03286	        for record in self.records:
03287	            if record.get('process_handle') is handle:
03288	                record['process_handle_closed'] = True
03289	                record['process_handle'] = None
03290	            if record.get('thread_handle') is handle:
03291	                record['thread_handle_closed'] = True
03292	                record['thread_handle'] = None
03293	        owner = item.get('owner_ref')
03294	        if owner is not None and getattr(owner, 'handle', None) is handle:
03295	            # The owner of the handle learns that it is closed.  The
03296	            # handle of the owner is the **original** handle and it is
03297	            # never closed twice.
03298	            try:
03299	                owner.handle = None
03300	                owner.closed = True
03301	            except Exception:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03302	                pass
03303	        self._release_resource(kind, handle)
03304	
03305	    def _terminate_the_job(self):
03306	        """Terminate the job of the round.
03307	
03308	        The termination happens once.  A repeated stop does not terminate
03309	        the job of the round a second time.
03310	        """
03311	        if self.job is None or self.job_terminated:
03312	            return
03313	        try:
03314	            self.win32.terminate_job(self.job, 1)
03315	            self.job_terminated = True
03316	        except BrokerError as error:
03317	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'terminate_job', 'code': error.code})
03318	
03319	    def _wait_the_processes(self):
03320	        """Wait for the original processes of the round.
03321	
03322	        Every process is independent of the others: a process that could
03323	        not be waited for does not skip the wait of the next one, and an
03324	        ordinary failure of the wait of one process does not interrupt
03325	        the group.  The wait is bounded by the ONE cleanup deadline of
03326	        the round and the remaining budget is recomputed before every
03327	        single wait: an expired deadline never starts a new wait.
03328	        """
03329	        for record in self.records:
03330	            try:
03331	                self._wait_one_process(record)
03332	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
03333	                # A cancellation never skips the processes that follow it:
03334	                # it is named and it is raised after every process was
03335	                # coordinated.
03336	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
03337	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__,
03338	                                      'cancellation': True})
03339	                self._processes_cancelled.append(error)
03340	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03341	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
03342	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03343	        if self._processes_cancelled:
03344	            raise self._processes_cancelled[0]
03345	
03346	    def _wait_one_process(self, record):
03347	        """Wait for one original process of the round, on its own."""
03348	        handle = record.get('process_handle')
03349	        if handle is None or record['exited']:
03350	            return
03351	        if not record['resumed']:
03352	            # The process never ran: it is stopped through its own
03353	            # suspended handle.
03354	            self._terminate_owned(handle, record, 'SUSPENDED_AT_STOP')
03355	            return
03356	        timeout = self._wait_timeout_ms()
03357	        if timeout <= 0:
03358	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
03359	                                  'code': 'BROKER_CLEANUP_DEADLINE_EXCEEDED'})
03360	            return
03361	        try:
03362	            record['exited'] = bool(self.win32.wait_process(handle, timeout))
03363	        except BrokerError as error:
03364	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
03365	                                  'code': error.code})
03366	        except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
03367	            # A cancellation is never turned into an ordinary evidence:
03368	            # it leaves the wait of this process and it is raised after
03369	            # every other process was coordinated.
03370	            raise
03371	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03372	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'wait', 'role': record['role'],
03373	                                  'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03374	            return
03375	        if record['exited']:
03376	            try:
03377	                record['exit_code'] = self.win32.exit_code(handle)
03378	            except BrokerError as error:
03379	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
03380	                                      'code': error.code})
03381	            except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit):
03382	                # The cancellation of the exit code is a cancellation of
03383	                # the round and it is never swallowed by the evidence.
03384	                raise
03385	            except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03386	                self.failures.append({'stage': 'exit_code', 'role': record['role'],
03387	                                      'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03388	
03389	    def _wait_timeout_ms(self):
03390	        """The milliseconds that are left until the cleanup deadline."""
03391	        remaining = self.remaining_cleanup_seconds()
03392	        if remaining <= 0:
03393	            return 0
03394	        return max(1, min(WAIT_MILLISECONDS, int(remaining * 1000)))
03395	
03396	    def _release_the_records(self):
03397	        """Close the original handles of the processes of the round.
03398	
03399	        A handle that was closed with a confirmed close is forgotten and
03400	        it is never closed a second time.  A handle that could not be
03401	        closed keeps its original reference and its owner.
03402	
03403	        The **control handle of a process** is closed **only** when the
03404	        exit of that process was really confirmed.  A process whose exit
03405	        was not confirmed - a suspended child that was never assigned to
03406	        the job of the round, a process whose wait timed out or whose
03407	        wait failed - may still be alive, and its control handle is the
03408	        only handle that may stop it.  The handle therefore stays with
03409	        the record of the process and with the pending table of the
03410	        round, and the same owner may confirm the exit and close it
03411	        later, inside the same cleanup deadline of the round.
03412	
03413	        The thread handle and the pipe handles are independent of the
03414	        exit of the process: they are closed on their own and they never
03415	        wait for the process.
03416	
03417	        Every handle of every record is closed on its own: a failure of
03418	        one of them never skips the handles that follow it, and a
03419	        cancellation is raised after every handle was coordinated.
03420	        """
03421	        cancellations = []
03422	        for record in self.records:
03423	            for key, kind in (('thread_handle', 'thread'),
03424	                              ('process_handle', 'process')):
03425	                handle = record.get(key)
03426	                if handle is None:
03427	                    continue
03428	                if kind == 'process' and not self._process_eligible(record):
03429	                    # The exit of the process was not confirmed: the
03430	                    # control handle stays with its owner and the round
03431	                    # keeps the fact.  The handle is never closed under
03432	                    # a process that nobody may stop any more.
03433	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
03434	                    self._retain_resource(
03435	                        kind, handle, 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED')
03436	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle',
03437	                                          'kind': kind,
03438	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
03439	                                          'code': 'BROKER_PROCESS_EXIT_NOT_CONFIRMED'})
03440	                    continue
03441	                try:
03442	                    closed = self._close_handle(kind, handle)
03443	                except (KeyboardInterrupt, SystemExit) as error:
03444	                    # A cancellation never skips the handles that follow
03445	                    # it: it is named and it is raised after every
03446	                    # handle was coordinated.
03447	                    record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = False
03448	                    self.failures.append({'stage': 'close_handle', 'kind': kind,
03449	                                          'role': record.get('role'),
03450	                                          'error_type': type(error).__name__,
03451	                                          'cancellation': True})
03452	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
03453	                    cancellations.append(error)
03454	                    continue
03455	                record[kind + '_handle_closed'] = closed
03456	                if closed:
03457	                    record[key] = None
03458	                else:
03459	                    self._retain_resource(kind, handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
03460	        if cancellations:
03461	            raise cancellations[0]
03462	
03463	    def _release_the_job(self):
03464	        """Close the original handle of the job of the round.
03465	
03466	        A job handle that could not be closed is **not** forgotten: the
03467	        core keeps the original reference and the round stays unknown.
03468	        """
03469	        if self.job is None:
03470	            return
03471	        handle = self.job
03472	        closed = self._close_handle('job', handle)
03473	        self.job_closed = closed
03474	        if closed:
03475	            self.job = None
03476	            self._release_resource('job', handle)
03477	        else:
03478	            self._retain_resource('job', handle, 'BROKER_HANDLE_CLOSE_FAILED')
03479	
03480	    def _is_released(self):
03481	        """Whether the round is a proven release.
03482	
03483	        A release needs every fact and not only the absence of a failure:
03484	        every owned process has to have exited, every handle has to have
03485	        been closed, every reader has to have been joined without a read
03486	        error and the job has to have been queried and found empty.
03487	        """
03488	        if self.failures:
03489	            return False
03490	        if not all(record['exited'] for record in self.records if record['created']):
03491	            return False
03492	        if not all(record['process_handle_closed'] and record['thread_handle_closed']
03493	                   for record in self.records):
03494	            return False
03495	        if not self.job_closed:
03496	            return False
03497	        if not (self.job_active_known and self.job_active == 0):
03498	            return False
03499	        if not self.reader_states:
03500	            return False
03501	        for state in self.reader_states:
03502	            if not state['finished'] or state['alive'] or state['error']:
03503	                return False
03504	        if self._pending.pending():
03505	            return False
03506	        return True
03507	
03508	    def _query_job(self):
03509	        """Ask the job how many processes it still holds.
03510	
03511	        The answer is bounded.  An unknown answer and an answer that is
03512	        over the cap are both *unknown*: neither of them is a zero.
03513	
03514	        The query only runs while the job handle is still open.  A
03515	        handle that was already closed keeps the answer that it produced:
03516	        a second stop never destroys the proof of the first one.
03517	        """
03518	        if self.job is None:
03519	            return
03520	        try:
03521	            identifiers = self.win32.job_process_ids(self.job)
03522	        except BrokerError as error:
03523	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': error.code})
03524	            self.job_active_known = False
03525	            self.job_active = None
03526	            return
03527	        if len(identifiers) > MAX_DESCENDANTS:
03528	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'query_job', 'code': 'BROKER_DESCENDANT_LIMIT'})
03529	            self.job_active_known = False
03530	            self.job_active = None
03531	            return
03532	        self.job_active_known = True
03533	        self.job_active = len(identifiers)
03534	        self.job_process_ids = list(identifiers)
03535	
03536	    def _collect_stream_counts(self):
03537	        total = 0
03538	        for record in self.records:
03539	            for stream, key in (('stdout', 'stdout_bytes'), ('stderr', 'stderr_bytes')):
03540	                for reader in self.readers:
03541	                    if reader.name.endswith(record['role'] + '-' + stream):
03542	                        record[key] = reader.observed
03543	                        record[stream + '_overflow'] = reader.overflow
03544	                        if reader.overflow and not self._stream_failure_reported:
03545	                            self._stream_failure_reported = True
03546	                            self.failures.append({'stage': 'stream_limit',
03547	                                                  'role': record['role'],
03548	                                                  'stream': stream,
03549	                                                  'code': 'BROKER_STREAM_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
03550	        for reader in self.readers:
03551	            total += reader.kept
03552	        self.new_text_bytes = total
03553	        if total > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES and not self._text_failure_reported:
03554	            self._text_failure_reported = True
03555	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'new_text_limit',
03556	                                  'code': 'BROKER_NEW_TEXT_LIMIT_EXCEEDED'})
03557	
03558	    def overflowed(self):
03559	        """Whether a bound of the text of the children was exceeded."""
03560	        self._collect_stream_counts()
03561	        if self.new_text_bytes > MAX_NEW_TEXT_BYTES:
03562	            return True
03563	        return any(record['stdout_overflow'] or record['stderr_overflow']
03564	                   for record in self.records)
03565	
03566	    def _abort_round(self, reason):
03567	        """Fail the round closed.
03568	
03569	        The stop of the round is bounded and it only touches the handles
03570	        and the job of this round.  The state of the round is unknown
03571	        afterwards, whatever the stop itself proved.  The abort is
03572	        idempotent: a failure that already aborted the round does not
03573	        abort it a second time and it does not report it twice.
03574	        """
03575	        if not self._abort_reported:
03576	            self._abort_reported = True
03577	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'round', 'code': reason})
03578	        try:
03579	            self.stop(reason, force_unknown=True)
03580	        except BrokerError as error:
03581	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'code': error.code})
03582	        except BaseException as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03583	            self.failures.append({'stage': 'abort', 'error_type': type(error).__name__})
03584	        self.state = 'UNKNOWN_RETAINED'
03585	
03586	    # -- the evidence ------------------------------------------------------
03587	    def summary(self):
03588	        """The bounded summary of the round that fits into one frame."""
03589	        return {'state': self.state,
03590	                'roles': [{'role': record['role'], 'pid': record['pid'],
03591	                           'created': record['created'], 'assigned': record['assigned'],
03592	                           'resumed': record['resumed'], 'exited': record['exited']}
03593	                          for record in self.records],
03594	                'job_active_known': self.job_active_known,
03595	                'job_active': self.job_active,
03596	                'failures': len(self.failures)}
03597	
03598	    def evidence(self):
03599	        return {
03600	            'kind': 'lvw.s3-native-broker-evidence/1',
03601	            'state': self.state,
03602	            'run_id': self.plan.run_id,
03603	            'candidate_sha': self.plan.candidate_sha,
03604	            'authorization_sha256': self.plan.authorization_sha256,
03605	            'grant_authority': 'NOT_VERIFIED',
03606	            'stop_reason': self.stop_reason,
03607	            'broker': self.broker_identity,
03608	            'job': {'created': self.job is not None or self.job_closed,
03609	                    'kill_on_job_close': self.job_kill_on_close,
03610	                    'breakaway_allowed': False,
03611	                    'terminated': self.job_terminated,
03612	                    'active_process_count': self.job_active,
03613	                    'active_process_count_known': self.job_active_known,
03614	                    'process_ids': list(self.job_process_ids),
03615	                    'handle_closed': self.job_closed},
03616	            'roles': [{key: value for key, value in record.items()
03617	                       if key not in ('process_handle', 'thread_handle')}
03618	                      for record in self.records],
03619	            'new_text_bytes': self.new_text_bytes,
03620	            'readers': list(self.reader_states),
03621	            'pending_resources': self._pending.pending_facts(),
03622	            # The resources that this round could not release and the
03623	            # original handles that the Windows façade still holds.  They
03624	            # are named with their original references: a resource that
03625	            # was not released is never replaced by a copy of a message.
03626	            'retained_resources': [{'kind': item['kind'], 'owner': item['owner'],
03627	                                    'code': item['code'],
03628	                                    'handle_repr': repr(item['handle'])}
03629	                                   for item in self._retained_resources],
03630	            'api_retained': self._api_retained(),
03631	            'cleanup_deadline': self._cleanup_deadline_value,
03632	            'failures': list(self.failures),
03633	            # The claims that this package does not make.
03634	            'native_execution_proven': False,
03635	            'native_cleanup_proven': False,
03636	            'gpu_released_proven': False,
03637	            'port_released_proven': False,
03638	            'sampler_proven': False,
03639	            'parent_crash_recovery_proven': False,
03640	            'windows_stdin_eof_proven': False,
03641	            'admission_produced': False,
03642	            'stage_pass_claim': False,
03643	            'product_gate0': False,
03644	        }
03645	
03646	
03647	# -- the bounded private pipe ----------------------------------------------
03648	
03649	def _require_deadline(deadline, code='BROKER_DEADLINE_INVALID'):
03650	    """Check one absolute deadline.
03651	
03652	    A deadline is a finite number of seconds of the monotonic clock of
03653	    the host.  A boolean, a string, an infinite value and a value that
03654	    is not a number at all are refused: a deadline that was understood
03655	    as something else would silently unbound the round.
03656	    """
03657	    if deadline is None:
03658	        return None
03659	    if isinstance(deadline, bool) or not isinstance(deadline, (int, float)):
03660	        raise BrokerError(code)
03661	    value = float(deadline)
03662	    if not math.isfinite(value):
03663	        raise BrokerError(code)
03664	    return value
03665	
03666	
03667	class _PosixSyscalls:
03668	    """The POSIX system calls of the private pipe.
03669	
03670	    The table is a narrow and explicit interface.  It is the only place
03671	    where the private channel touches the operating system, and it can
03672	    be replaced by a synthetic table in the offline validation: the
03673	    production logic of the channel is then driven by a table that
03674	    records the calls instead of by the kernel.
03675	    """
03676	
03677	    def __init__(self):
03678	        self._pollers = {}
03679	
03680	    def fileno(self, file_object):
03681	        return file_object.fileno()
03682	
03683	    def set_blocking(self, descriptor, blocking):
03684	        os.set_blocking(descriptor, blocking)
03685	
03686	    def _poller(self, descriptor):
03687	        poller = self._pollers.get(descriptor)
03688	        if poller is None:
03689	            poller = select.poll()
03690	            poller.register(descriptor,
03691	                            select.POLLIN | select.POLLOUT
03692	                            | select.POLLERR | select.POLLHUP)
03693	            self._pollers[descriptor] = poller
03694	        return poller
03695	
03696	    def poll(self, descriptor, mask, timeout):
03697	        """Whether the descriptor is ready for the requested operation."""
03698	        ready = self._poller(descriptor).poll(max(0.0, timeout) * 1000)
03699	        for number, event in ready:
03700	            if event & select.POLLNVAL:
03701	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_DESCRIPTOR_INVALID')
03702	            if mask == 'read' and event & (select.POLLIN | select.POLLHUP
03703	                                           | select.POLLERR):
03704	                return True
03705	            if mask == 'write' and event & (select.POLLOUT | select.POLLHUP
03706	                                            | select.POLLERR):
03707	                return True
03708	        return False
03709	
03710	    def read(self, descriptor, size):
03711	        return os.read(descriptor, size)
03712	
03713	    def write(self, descriptor, data):
03714	        return os.write(descriptor, data)
03715	
03716	    def close(self, file_object):
03717	        file_object.close()
03718	
03719	
03720	class _PosixPipeEndpoint:
03721	    """One end of a private pipe on a POSIX host.
03722	
03723	    The endpoint owns the original descriptor of the pipe and it closes
03724	    it itself.  The descriptor is set to non blocking exactly once, by
03725	    the owner that created it, and the read and the write poll it in
03726	    slices of at most 50 milliseconds.  Every slice checks the cancel
03727	    flag and the absolute deadline, so that a peer that never reads can
03728	    never hold the supervisor of the round.
03729	
03730	    The endpoint never changes the blocking mode of a descriptor that it
03731	    does not own and it never closes a descriptor that it does not own.
03732	    It never relies on the lock of a buffered object either: the close
03733	    of a buffered object may wait for a reader that holds its lock, and
03734	    the close of this endpoint is the close of the owner of the
03735	    descriptor.
03736	    """
03737	
03738	    def __init__(self, file_object, *, name, syscalls=None, clock=None):
03739	        self._file = file_object
03740	        self._name = name
03741	        self._syscalls = syscalls or _PosixSyscalls()
03742	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
03743	        self._descriptor = self._syscalls.fileno(file_object)
03744	        self._non_blocking = False
03745	        self._closed = False
03746	        # The absolute deadline that is bound to this endpoint.  It may
03747	        # only shorten the deadline that is already bound and it is read
03748	        # again on every slice: a deadline that was bound after a read
03749	        # started still tightens that read.
03750	        self._deadline = None
03751	        self.facts = {'name': name, 'owner': 'POSIX_ENDPOINT',
03752	                      'descriptor': self._descriptor}
03753	
03754	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
03755	        """Bind an absolute deadline to this endpoint.
03756	
03757	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
03758	        bound.  It is a property of the endpoint and not a local
03759	        argument of one call: a call that passed ``None`` before the
03760	        binding still observes the bound deadline.
03761	        """
03762	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
03763	        if value is None:
03764	            return self._deadline
03765	        if self._deadline is not None and value > self._deadline:
03766	            return self._deadline
03767	        self._deadline = value
03768	        return value
03769	
03770	    def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
03771	        """The deadline that this call really uses."""
03772	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
03773	        if self._deadline is None:
03774	            return value
03775	        if value is None:
03776	            return self._deadline
03777	        return min(value, self._deadline)
03778	
03779	    def prepare(self):
03780	        """Set the descriptor to non blocking, once, by its owner."""
03781	        if self._non_blocking or self._closed:
03782	            return
03783	        self._syscalls.set_blocking(self._descriptor, False)
03784	        self._non_blocking = True
03785	        self.facts['non_blocking'] = True
03786	
03787	    def _check(self, deadline, cancel):
03788	        if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
03789	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
03790	        if deadline is not None and self._clock() >= deadline:
03791	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
03792	
03793	    def read(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
03794	        """Read one block of the pipe, bounded by the deadline."""
03795	        self.prepare()
03796	        while True:
03797	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
03798	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'read',
03799	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
03800	                continue
03801	            try:
03802	                data = self._syscalls.read(self._descriptor, PIPE_READ_BYTES)
03803	            except BlockingIOError:
03804	                # The pipe was not ready after all: the slice is repeated.
03805	                continue
03806	            except OSError as error:
03807	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_READ_FAILED',
03808	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
03809	            if not data:
03810	                return b''
03811	            return data
03812	
03813	    def write(self, data, deadline=None, cancel=None):
03814	        """Write the whole block, bounded by the deadline."""
03815	        self.prepare()
03816	        view = memoryview(bytes(data))
03817	        total = 0
03818	        while total < len(view):
03819	            self._check(self._effective_deadline(deadline), cancel)
03820	            if not self._syscalls.poll(self._descriptor, 'write',
03821	                                       POLL_SLICE_SECONDS):
03822	                continue
03823	            try:
03824	                count = self._syscalls.write(self._descriptor, view[total:])
03825	            except BlockingIOError:
03826	                # The pipe was full after all: the slice is repeated.
03827	                continue
03828	            except OSError as error:
03829	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED',
03830	                                  '%s:%s' % (self._name, type(error).__name__))
03831	            if count <= 0:
03832	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_WRITE_FAILED', self._name)
03833	            total += count
03834	        return total
03835	
03836	    def close(self):
03837	        """Close the original descriptor of this endpoint, exactly once.
03838	
03839	        The endpoint is closed **only** when the close really happened.
03840	        A close that failed keeps the descriptor and its owner: the
03841	        endpoint may try it again and it is never marked as closed
03842	        irreversibly.
03843	
03844	        A close of an endpoint that is already closed keeps the fact
03845	        that it is closed: the second close is idempotent and it never
03846	        turns a proven close into a missing one.
03847	        """
03848	        if self._closed:
03849	            self.facts['closed'] = True
03850	            self.facts['already_closed'] = True
03851	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
03852	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
03853	            return self.facts
03854	        try:
03855	            self._syscalls.close(self._file)
03856	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
03857	            self.facts['closed'] = False
03858	            self.facts['error_type'] = type(error).__name__
03859	            self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
03860	            self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
03861	            return self.facts
03862	        self._closed = True
03863	        self.facts['closed'] = True
03864	        # This host has no worker thread for an end of the pipe: the
03865	        # blocking calls are made by the reader of the channel itself.
03866	        # There is therefore no original worker handle that could be
03867	        # outstanding, and the fact says so.
03868	        self.facts['retained_workers'] = 0
03869	        self.facts['worker_thread_handle_released'] = True
03870	        return self.facts
03871	
03872	
03873	class _WindowsPipeWorker(threading.Thread):
03874	    """One worker of one end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
03875	
03876	    The worker owns one blocking operation at a time.  It takes the
03877	    request from its queue, it performs the blocking call on the
03878	    original handle of the pipe and it reports the real completion of
03879	    the call.  The thread is created once for the endpoint and it is
03880	    never created for a frame.
03881	
03882	    The handle of the thread is the handle of the thread itself and
03883	    never a self reported identifier: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` needs the
03884	    original handle of the thread that performs the operation.
03885	
03886	    The lifecycle of the worker is **explicit**.  The states are
03887	    ``IDLE``, ``QUEUED``, ``IN_IO``, ``CANCELLING``, ``DONE`` and
03888	    ``STOPPED``:
03889	
03890	    * ``IDLE``      the worker waits for a request;
03891	    * ``QUEUED``    a request waits for the worker;
03892	    * ``IN_IO``     the worker is inside the blocking call;
03893	    * ``CANCELLING`` a cancellation was asked for;
03894	    * ``DONE``      the request of the worker completed;
03895	    * ``STOPPED``   the thread of the worker really ended.
03896	
03897	    ``DONE`` is not ``STOPPED``: the completion of a request says
03898	    nothing about the thread of the worker, and the handle of the
03899	    thread may only be closed after the thread really ended.
03900	
03901	    The worker is the **only** owner of the handle of its own thread:
03902	    it duplicated that handle and it closes it when its thread really
03903	    ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle and its owner:
03904	    the handle is never closed under a live worker and it is never left
03905	    to the garbage collector.
03906	    """
03907	
03908	    STATES = ('IDLE', 'QUEUED', 'IN_IO', 'CANCELLING', 'DONE', 'STOPPED')
03909	
03910	    def __init__(self, api, handle, *, kind, action, name, clock=None,
03911	                 context=None):
03912	        super().__init__(daemon=True, name=name)
03913	        self.api = api
03914	        self.handle = handle
03915	        self.kind = kind
03916	        self.action = action
03917	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
03918	        self._requests = queue.Queue(maxsize=1)
03919	        # The event of the in-flight operation.  It is set while the
03920	        # worker is inside the call and it is cleared again when the
03921	        # call ended: an event that is never cleared would report a
03922	        # permanent in-flight state.
03923	        self.inside = threading.Event()
03924	        self.done = threading.Event()
03925	        self.stopped = threading.Event()
03926	        self.ready = threading.Event()
03927	        self.result = None
03928	        self.error = None
03929	        # The **original** error object of the last request.  The code
03930	        # of the error is a name; the object is the failure.  It is kept
03931	        # so that the endpoint may raise the very object that the
03932	        # primitive raised instead of a copy of its name.
03933	        self._error_object = None
03934	        self.thread_handle = None
03935	        self.thread_handle_released = False
03936	        self.joined = False
03937	        self.state = 'IDLE'
03938	        # The number of the requests that were submitted and the number
03939	        # of the requests that completed.  The two numbers are the real
03940	        # completion of a request: the event alone could report the
03941	        # completion of an older request.
03942	        self._condition = threading.Condition()
03943	        self._submitted = 0
03944	        self._completed = 0
03945	        # The generations of the requests that were handed over, in the
03946	        # order in which they were handed over.  The worker takes the
03947	        # request and its generation together: a worker that would read
03948	        # the number of the submissions would name the generation of a
03949	        # later request.
03950	        self._generations = collections.deque()
03951	        # The **outcome of every generation**.  The outcome carries the
03952	        # result, the error, the **original error object**, the source
03953	        # of the failure and the order of the completion.  A result
03954	        # that was really published is never replaced by a later one and
03955	        # a cancellation never rewrites the outcome of a generation.
03956	        self._outcomes = {}
03957	        # The **admission guard of every generation**: the cancel flag
03958	        # and the absolute deadline that the request of the generation
03959	        # carries.  The guard is the admission of the worker itself: a
03960	        # request whose cancel was published or whose deadline arrived
03961	        # **before** the worker entered the operation performs **no**
03962	        # primitive call at all.  The guard travels with the request
03963	        # and it is never a deadline of the worker.
03964	        self._guards = {}
03965	        # The explicit notification that a **failure** of a generation
03966	        # was really published.  It is set under the lock of the worker
03967	        # and it is set **after** the outcome was recorded: an owner
03968	        # that waits for it knows that the failure of the round is
03969	        # published and that a later cancellation may not replace it.
03970	        self.outcome_published = threading.Event()
03971	        # The order of the events of this worker.  Every completion and
03972	        # every cancellation takes a number of it, under the lock of the
03973	        # worker: the number tells whether a failure was published
03974	        # **before** the cancellation of the round or after it.
03975	        self._sequence = 0
03976	        self._cancel_sequence = None
03977	        # The ONE context of the round.  The worker reads the absolute
03978	        # deadline and the published cleanup deadline of the round from
03979	        # it and it never keeps a budget of its own.
03980	        self._context = context
03981	        # The flag of the cancellation.  It stops the worker from
03982	        # accepting a new task before the cancellation is requested.
03983	        self._cancelled = False
03984	        # The name of the cancel flag is not ``_stop``: the base class of
03985	        # the thread uses that name for its own method and a shadow of it
03986	        # would break the join of the thread.
03987	        self._stop_event = threading.Event()
03988	        # The pause of the retry of a cancellation.  It is its **own**
03989	        # event and it is never set: the retry of a cancellation may not
03990	        # wait on the stop event of the worker, because the worker set
03991	        # that event itself and the wait would return at once.
03992	        self._pause = threading.Event()
03993	
03994	    # -- the lifecycle ----------------------------------------------------
03995	    def run(self):
03996	        try:
03997	            self.thread_handle = self.api.duplicate_current_thread_handle()
03998	        except BrokerError as error:
03999	            self.error = error.code
04000	            self.done.set()
04001	            self.ready.set()
04002	            self.state = 'DONE'
04003	            self.stopped.set()
04004	            return
04005	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
04006	            self.error = type(error).__name__
04007	            self.done.set()
04008	            self.ready.set()
04009	            self.state = 'DONE'
04010	            self.stopped.set()
04011	            return
04012	        self.ready.set()
04013	        try:
04014	            while not self._stop_event.is_set():
04015	                try:
04016	                    request = self._requests.get(timeout=0.1)
04017	                except queue.Empty:
04018	                    continue
04019	                with self._condition:
04020	                    generation = (self._generations.popleft()
04021	                                  if self._generations else self._submitted)
04022	                self.error = None
04023	                self.result = None
04024	                self._error_object = None
04025	                # The check of the cancellation and the publication of
04026	                # the intent to enter the operation are **one** atomic
04027	                # step under the lock of the worker.  A cancellation
04028	                # that arrives between the check and the operation would
04029	                # otherwise be answered by a worker that performs the
04030	                # operation anyway: the cancellation and the operation
04031	                # would be two uncoordinated facts.
04032	                #
04033	                # The **admission guard of the generation** is asked in
04034	                # the same atomic step: the cancel flag and the
04035	                # deadline of the request travel with the request and
04036	                # the worker never enters the operation of a request
04037	                # whose admission was already refused.
04038	                with self._condition:
04039	                    cancelled = self._cancelled
04040	                    if not cancelled:
04041	                        guard = self._guards.get(generation)
04042	                        if guard is not None:
04043	                            guard_cancel, guard_deadline = guard
04044	                            if guard_cancel is not None and guard_cancel.is_set():
04045	                                cancelled = True
04046	                                self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED'
04047	                                # The error of the refusal carries the
04048	                                # owner of the cancel flag, exactly like
04049	                                # the refusal of the endpoint: a
04050	                                # cancellation that the close of the
04051	                                # channel initiated is a named fact of
04052	                                # that close and it is never the error
04053	                                # of the reader.
04054	                                self._error_object = _cancel_source_error(
04055	                                    BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED',
04056	                                                self.kind), guard_cancel)
04057	                            elif (guard_deadline is not None
04058	                                  and self._clock() >= guard_deadline):
04059	                                cancelled = True
04060	                                self.error = 'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT'
04061	                                self._error_object = BrokerError(
04062	                                    'BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self.kind)
04063	                    if not cancelled:
04064	                        self.state = 'IN_IO'
04065	                        self.inside.set()
04066	                if cancelled:
04067	                    # The cancellation reached the worker before the
04068	                    # operation started: the operation is not performed
04069	                    # at all and the request is answered as finished.
04070	                    self._complete(generation)
04071	                    continue
04072	                try:
04073	                    self.result = self.action(request)
04074	                except BrokerError as error:
04075	                    self.error = error.code
04076	                    self._error_object = error
04077	                except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
04078	                    self.error = type(error).__name__
04079	                    self._error_object = error
04080	                finally:
04081	                    self.inside.clear()
04082	                    self._complete(generation)
04083	        finally:
04084	            self.state = 'STOPPED'
04085	            self.stopped.set()
04086	            # The worker is the only owner of the handle of its own
04087	            # thread and it closes it here, when its thread really ended.
04088	            self.release_thread_handle()
04089	
04090	    def _complete(self, generation=None):
04091	        """Report the real completion of the current request.
04092	
04093	        The outcome of the generation is **recorded** here, under the
04094	        lock of the worker: the result, the code of the error, the
04095	        **original error object** and the order of the completion.  The
04096	        record is the history of the request and it is never replaced
04097	        by a later one.
04098	
04099	        The order of the completion is the order of the events of the
04100	        worker: it tells whether a failure was published **before** the
04101	        cancellation of the round or after it.  A failure that was
04102	        published before the cancellation is a fault of the round and it
04103	        stays the first error; a failure that was published after the
04104	        cancellation is the consequence of the cancellation.
04105	        """
04106	        with self._condition:
04107	            self._completed += 1
04108	            self._sequence += 1
04109	            if generation is not None:
04110	                record = {
04111	                    'generation': generation,
04112	                    'result': self.result,
04113	                    'error': self.error,
04114	                    'error_object': self._error_object,
04115	                    'source': 'WORKER',
04116	                    'sequence': self._sequence,
04117	                    'cancel_sequence': self._cancel_sequence,
04118	                }
04119	                self._outcomes[generation] = record
04120	                if record['error'] is not None:
04121	                    # The failure of this generation is published: a
04122	                    # later cancellation of the round may not replace
04123	                    # it.
04124	                    self.outcome_published.set()
04125	            self._condition.notify_all()
04126	        self.done.set()
04127	        self.state = 'DONE'
04128	
04129	    def outcome(self, generation):
04130	        """The recorded outcome of one generation of this worker.
04131	
04132	        The outcome is the **original** outcome of the request: the
04133	        result, the error and the error object of the request that
04134	        really ran.  It is ``None`` when the request did not complete.
04135	        """
04136	        with self._condition:
04137	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
04138	            return dict(record) if record is not None else None
04139	
04140	    def published_before_cancel(self, generation):
04141	        """Whether the outcome of this generation was published first.
04142	
04143	        The comparison of the two orders is the only proof that a
04144	        failure of the round happened **before** the cancellation of the
04145	        round.  A cancellation that was requested first may not rewrite
04146	        the failure of a request that was still running, and a failure
04147	        that was published first may never be replaced by a
04148	        cancellation.
04149	
04150	        A worker that was never cancelled proves the order by itself:
04151	        the failure was published before any cancellation of it.
04152	        """
04153	        with self._condition:
04154	            record = self._outcomes.get(generation)
04155	            if record is None or record.get('error') is None:
04156	                return False
04157	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
04158	                return True
04159	            return record['sequence'] < self._cancel_sequence
04160	
04161	    def _request_cancellation(self):
04162	        """Take the order of the cancellation of this worker.
04163	
04164	        The order is taken under the lock of the worker, so that the
04165	        comparison with the order of a completion is a real order and
04166	        not a guess of the scheduler.
04167	        """
04168	        with self._condition:
04169	            self._cancelled = True
04170	            if self._cancel_sequence is None:
04171	                self._sequence += 1
04172	                self._cancel_sequence = self._sequence
04173	            self.state = 'CANCELLING'
04174	            self._condition.notify_all()
04175	        self._stop_event.set()
04176	
04177	    def wait_ready(self, seconds):
04178	        """Wait for the worker to have acquired the handle of its thread."""
04179	        return self.ready.wait(max(0.0, seconds))
04180	
04181	    def submit(self, request, cancel=None, deadline=None):
04182	        """Hand one request to the worker.
04183	
04184	        The hand over is bounded: the queue of the worker holds one
04185	        request and a request that does not fit is refused with a named
04186	        error.  A worker that is dead and a worker whose thread handle
04187	        could not be created refuse the request as well.
04188	
04189	        The hand over and the cancellation share **one** lock: a
04190	        request that is submitted after the cancellation of the worker
04191	        is refused and it never enters the queue.  The cancellation and
04192	        the submission are therefore coordinated by the same generation
04193	        of the worker and a request of an older generation is never
04194	        taken for the completion of this one.
04195	
04196	        The **admission guard** of the request - the cancel flag and the
04197	        absolute deadline of the call - travels with the request: the
04198	        worker asks it before it enters the operation and a request
04199	        whose admission was refused performs **no** primitive call.
04200	
04201	        The completion of a request is the number of the completed
04202	        requests and not the event alone: the completion of an older
04203	        request is never the completion of this one.
04204	        """
04205	        if not self.is_alive():
04206	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_ALIVE', self.kind)
04207	        if self.thread_handle is None:
04208	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_NOT_READY',
04209	                               '%s:%s' % (self.kind, self.error))
04210	        with self._condition:
04211	            if self._cancelled:
04212	                # The cancellation of this worker arrived first: the
04213	                # request is refused and it never enters the queue.
04214	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_CANCELLED', self.kind)
04215	            self._submitted += 1
04216	            generation = self._submitted
04217	            # The generation travels with the request: the worker takes
04218	            # the request and the generation of **that** request.  The
04219	            # generation is registered **before** the request is handed
04220	            # over: a worker that takes the request never finds an
04221	            # empty list of generations.
04222	            self._generations.append(generation)
04223	            self._guards[generation] = (cancel, deadline)
04224	            try:
04225	                self._requests.put(request, timeout=POLL_SLICE_SECONDS)
04226	            except queue.Full:
04227	                self._submitted -= 1
04228	                self._guards.pop(generation, None)
04229	                if self._generations and self._generations[-1] == generation:
04230	                    self._generations.pop()
04231	                self._condition.notify_all()
04232	                raise BrokerError('BROKER_WORKER_QUEUE_FULL', self.kind)
04233	            self.done.clear()
04234	            self.state = 'QUEUED'
04235	            return generation
04236	
04237	    def wait_done(self, generation, timeout):
04238	        """Wait for the completion of one request, bounded."""
04239	        with self._condition:
04240	            return self._condition.wait_for(
04241	                lambda: self._completed >= generation, max(0.0, timeout))
04242	
04243	    def request_cancel(self, deadline=None):
04244	        """Ask for the cancellation of the operation of this worker.
04245	
04246	        The request is not a completion.  The caller has to wait for the
04247	        real completion of the worker and to join it.
04248	
04249	        The cancellation stops the worker from accepting a new task
04250	        **first** and it drives the worker to its end: the worker that
04251	        is inside an operation is asked to leave it and the worker that
04252	        waits for a request is asked to stop waiting.  A cancellation
04253	        that reports ``NOT_FOUND`` is repeated, bounded by the shared
04254	        deadline, until the worker reports that **this** generation is
04255	        done or that the thread really stopped: a cancellation that was
04256	        sent once and dropped is not a cancellation.
04257	
04258	        The retry never waits on the stop event of the worker: the
04259	        worker set that event itself, a moment before, and a wait that
04260	        returns at once would end the retry after a single
04261	        ``NOT_FOUND``.  The retry waits on its own pause instead, so
04262	        that the request of the operating system is really repeated.
04263	
04264	        The completion of an **older** generation never releases the
04265	        retry: the real completion of the request of this generation is
04266	        the number of the completed requests.
04267	
04268	        The deadline of the retry is **recomputed on every round** of
04269	        the loop: a deadline that was published after the retry started
04270	        still shortens it, and the pause of the retry is cropped to the
04271	        time that is really left.  A retry that used the number of the
04272	        deadline that it found when it started would wait longer than
04273	        the round allows.
04274	        """
04275	        self._request_cancellation()
04276	        if self.thread_handle is None:
04277	            return 'NO_HANDLE'
04278	        # The bound of the retry is fixed **once**: a retry that used a
04279	        # deadline that moved with every round would never expire.
04280	        bound = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
04281	        if bound is None:
04282	            # The retry of a cancellation is **always** bounded: a
04283	            # worker that never ends may not hold the caller for ever.
04284	            # The bound of the package is used only when neither the
04285	            # caller nor the context of the round brought a deadline.
04286	            bound = self._clock() + CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
04287	        while True:
04288	            try:
04289	                state = self.api.cancel_synchronous_io(self.thread_handle)
04290	            except BrokerError as error:
04291	                self.error = error.code
04292	                return 'FAILED'
04293	            except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
04294	                self.error = type(error).__name__
04295	                return 'FAILED'
04296	            if state != 'NOT_FOUND':
04297	                return state
04298	            # The operation was not found: it had not started yet or it
04299	            # had just finished.  The worker is asked again until the
04300	            # request of this generation completed or until the thread
04301	            # of the worker really stopped.
04302	            with self._condition:
04303	                completed = self._completed >= self._submitted
04304	            if completed or self.stopped.is_set():
04305	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
04306	            # The deadline of this round of the retry is read **again**
04307	            # from the call and from the context of the round: a
04308	            # deadline that was published after the retry started still
04309	            # shortens the retry that is running.
04310	            effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
04311	            if effective is None or bound < effective:
04312	                effective = bound
04313	            if self._clock() >= effective:
04314	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
04315	            # The pause of the retry is its own event: the stop event of
04316	            # the worker was set by this very method and it may never be
04317	            # used as the sleep of the retry.  The pause is cropped to
04318	            # the time that is really left.
04319	            pause = POLL_SLICE_SECONDS
04320	            if effective is not None:
04321	                pause = max(0.0, min(pause, effective - self._clock()))
04322	            if pause <= 0:
04323	                return 'NOT_FOUND'
04324	            self._pause.wait(pause)
04325	
04326	    def _effective_deadline(self, call=None):
04327	        """The ONE deadline that a wait of this worker really uses.
04328	
04329	        The deadline of the call, the absolute deadline of the round and
04330	        the **published** cleanup deadline of the round are one value:
04331	        the earliest of them.  The value is recomputed on every call.
04332	        """
04333	        value = _require_deadline(call)
04334	        context = self._context
04335	        if context is not None:
04336	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
04337	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
04338	                value = absolute
04339	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
04340	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
04341	                value = cleanup
04342	        return value
04343	
04344	    def stop(self):
04345	        """Ask the worker to end its loop."""
04346	        self._stop_event.set()
04347	
04348	    def stop_accepting(self):
04349	        """Stop the worker from accepting a new task.
04350	
04351	        The flag is written under the **same** lock that the worker of
04352	        the endpoint reads it under: the cancellation and the intent to
04353	        enter the operation are one atomic step.
04354	
04355	        The **order** of the cancellation is taken here as well: a
04356	        failure that was published before this call is a fault of the
04357	        round and a cancellation may never replace it.
04358	        """
04359	        self._request_cancellation()
04360	
04361	    def release_thread_handle(self):
04362	        """Close the handle of the thread of this worker.
04363	
04364	        The worker is the only owner of that handle.  It is closed here
04365	        and only here, and only after the thread of the worker really
04366	        ended.  A worker that did not stop keeps its handle.
04367	        """
04368	        if self.thread_handle is None:
04369	            return True
04370	        handle = self.thread_handle
04371	        try:
04372	            self.api.close_handle(handle)
04373	        except BrokerError as error:
04374	            self.error = error.code
04375	            return False
04376	        except Exception as error:  # noqa: BLE001 - the failure stays visible
04377	            self.error = type(error).__name__
04378	            return False
04379	        self.thread_handle = None
04380	        self.thread_handle_released = True
04381	        return True
04382	
04383	    def join_bounded(self, seconds):
04384	        """Join the worker and report whether it really stopped.
04385	
04386	        A worker whose thread was never started cannot be joined: the
04387	        join of such a thread is an error of the caller and not a fact
04388	        of the round.  The join reports that the worker did not stop
04389	        and it never pretends that the thread ended.
04390	        """
04391	        try:
04392	            self.join(max(0.0, seconds))
04393	        except RuntimeError:
04394	            # The thread was never started: it is not a thread of the
04395	            # round that ended, it is a thread that never came up.
04396	            self.joined = False
04397	            return False
04398	        self.joined = not self.is_alive()
04399	        return self.joined
04400	
04401	
04402	class _WindowsPipeEndpoint:
04403	    """One end of a private pipe on a Windows host.
04404	
04405	    The read and the write of the endpoint each own exactly one worker
04406	    thread and there is never more than one of them: the number of the
04407	    threads of a channel is fixed and it never grows with the number of
04408	    the frames.
04409	
04410	    The supervisor never performs the blocking call itself.  It waits
04411	    for the completion of the worker in bounded slices, it checks the
04412	    cancel flag and the deadline on every slice and it cancels the
04413	    worker through the original handle of its thread when the deadline
04414	    arrives.  The worker that is inside the call is the one that may
04415	    block; the supervisor never does.
04416	
04417	    The cancellation of a synchronous operation is a request and not a
04418	    completion: ``CancelSynchronousIo`` may report that the operation
04419	    was not found because it had not started yet or because it had just
04420	    finished.  The worker therefore reports the real state of its
04421	    operation, the supervisor joins it with a bounded wait, and a worker
04422	    that did not stop keeps its owner reference and makes the round
04423	    unknown.
04424	
04425	    A write is a **complete** write: the endpoint writes the whole
04426	    block, in as many partial writes as Windows needs, and it reports
04427	    the number of the bytes that really went into the pipe.  A partial
04428	    write is never reported as a success.
04429	    """
04430	
04431	    #: The bound absolute deadline of this endpoint.  It is a class
04432	    #: attribute so that an endpoint that was built without its
04433	    #: constructor still has a real deadline and never a missing one.
04434	    _deadline = None
04435	    #: The cancel flag and the deadline of the write that is in flight.
04436	    #: They are class attributes for the same reason as the deadline
04437	    #: above: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
04438	    #: has them and never a missing one.
04439	    _write_cancel = None
04440	    _write_deadline = None
04441	    #: The context of the round.  It is a class attribute for the same
04442	    #: reason: an endpoint that was built without its constructor still
04443	    #: reads the absolute deadline of the round from it and it never
04444	    #: treats a missing context as an unlimited budget.
04445	    _context = None
04446	    #: The lock of the **admission** of the endpoint.  It is a class
04447	    #: attribute for the same reason: an endpoint that was built without
04448	    #: its constructor still has a **real** admission and it never has a
04449	    #: permissive one.  The lock is created **per instance** on its
04450	    #: first use and it is never shared between two endpoints.
04451	    _admission_lock = None
04452	
04453	    def __init__(self, file_object, *, name, api=None, clock=None,
04454	                 handle=None, context=None):
04455	        self._file = file_object
04456	        self._name = name
04457	        self._api = api
04458	        self._clock = clock or time.monotonic
04459	        self._closed = False
04460	        self._reader = None
04461	        self._writer = None
04462	        self._retained = []
04463	        self._deadline = None
04464	        self._write_cancel = None
04465	        self._write_deadline = None
04466	        # The lock of the **admission** of the endpoint.  The check of
04467	        # the cancel flag, of the deadline of the round and the entry of
04468	        # the request into the worker are one atomic step under it.
04469	        self._admission_lock = threading.RLock()
04470	        # The ONE context of the round.  The endpoint reads the absolute
04471	        # deadline of the round from it and it never keeps a budget of
04472	        # its own.
04473	        self._context = context
04474	        if handle is None:
04475	            import msvcrt
04476	            handle = msvcrt.get_osfhandle(file_object.fileno())
04477	        self._handle = handle
04478	        self.facts = {'name': name, 'owner': 'WINDOWS_ENDPOINT'}
04479	
04480	    def bind_deadline(self, deadline):
04481	        """Bind an absolute deadline to this endpoint.
04482	
04483	        The deadline may only shorten the deadline that is already
04484	        bound and it is read again on every slice.
04485	        """
04486	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
04487	        if value is None:
04488	            return self._deadline
04489	        if self._deadline is not None and value > self._deadline:
04490	            return self._deadline
04491	        self._deadline = value
04492	        return value
04493	
04494	    def bind_context(self, context):
04495	        """Bind the ONE context of the round to this endpoint."""
04496	        if context is not None:
04497	            self._context = context
04498	        return self._context
04499	
04500	    def _effective_deadline(self, deadline=None):
04501	        """The deadline that this call really uses.
04502	
04503	        The deadline of the call, the deadline that is bound to the
04504	        endpoint, the absolute deadline of the round and the **published**
04505	        cleanup deadline of the round are **one** value: the earliest of
04506	        the four.  The endpoint never keeps a deadline of its own and it
04507	        never treats a missing context as an unlimited budget.
04508	
04509	        The published cleanup deadline is part of it: a round that
04510	        entered its cleanup publishes one deadline and the endpoint
04511	        that reads the context afterwards is bound by it.  An endpoint
04512	        that still waited with the long lease of the round would
04513	        outlive the only cleanup budget of the round.
04514	        """
04515	        value = _require_deadline(deadline)
04516	        bound = self._deadline
04517	        if bound is not None and (value is None or bound < value):
04518	            value = bound
04519	        context = self._context
04520	        if context is not None:
04521	            absolute = context.absolute_deadline
04522	            if absolute is not None and (value is None or absolute < value):
04523	                value = absolute
04524	            cleanup = context.cleanup_deadline_value()
04525	            if cleanup is not None and (value is None or cleanup < value):
04526	                value = cleanup
04527	        return value
04528	
04529	    def _admission_lock_for(self):
04530	        """The lock of the admission of **this** endpoint.
04531	
04532	        The lock is created **per instance** and it is created once.
04533	        An endpoint that was built without its constructor - a fixture
04534	        of the offline validation - therefore still has a **real**
04535	        admission: the check of the cancel flag and of the deadline is
04536	        never skipped and it is never permissive.  The creation of the
04537	        lock is guarded by one lock of the module, so that two threads
04538	        that arrive at the same time never build two locks for the same
04539	        endpoint.
04540	        """
04541	        lock = self.__dict__.get('_admission_lock')
04542	        if lock is not None:
04543	            return lock
04544	        with _ADMISSION_LOCK_INIT:
04545	            lock = self.__dict__.get('_admission_lock')
04546	            if lock is None:
04547	                lock = threading.RLock()
04548	                self.__dict__['_admission_lock'] = lock
04549	        return lock
04550	
04551	    def _admission(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
04552	        """The admission of one generation of this endpoint.
04553	
04554	        The admission is asked **before** every side effect: before the
04555	        worker of the endpoint is started, before a request is
04556	        submitted and before the primitive is called.  A call that
04557	        entered when the round was already expired or already cancelled
04558	        is refused there: it never starts a worker, it never submits a
04559	        request and it never calls the primitive.
04560	
04561	        The check and the admission of the generation are **one atomic
04562	        step** under the lock of the endpoint: a cancellation that
04563	        arrives between the check and the submit may not be answered by
04564	        a request that was submitted anyway.
04565	        """
04566	        with self._admission_lock_for():
04567	            return self._admission_locked(deadline, cancel)
04568	
04569	    def _admission_locked(self, deadline=None, cancel=None):
04570	        """The admission of one generation, under the lock of the endpoint.
04571	
04572	        The caller holds the lock of the admission: the check of the
04573	        cancel flag, of the deadline of the round and of the context of
04574	        the round and the decision of the admission are **one** atomic
04575	        step.  The **final** admission of a generation is asked here,
04576	        immediately before the submission of the request: a
04577	        cancellation or an expiry that was published **before** that
04578	        admission causes **zero** primitive calls.
04579	        """
04580	        effective = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
04581	        if cancel is not None and cancel.is_set():
04582	            # The cancellation of the round arrived first: the
04583	            # request is refused and no side effect happens.
04584	            raise _cancel_source_error(
04585	                BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name), cancel)
04586	        context = self._context
04587	        if context is not None and context.cancel.is_set():
04588	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_CANCELLED', self._name)
04589	        if effective is not None and self._clock() >= effective:
04590	            raise BrokerError('BROKER_PIPE_TIMEOUT', self._name)
04591	        return effective
04592	
04593	    def _remaining(self, deadline=None):
04594	        """The seconds that are left until the deadline of this endpoint."""
04595	        value = self._effective_deadline(deadline)
04596	        if value is None:
04597	            return CLOSE_JOIN_SECONDS
04598	        return max(0.0, value - self._clock())
04599	
04600	    def _start(self, kind, action, deadline=None):
04601	        """Create and start one worker of this endpoint.
04602	
04603	        The worker is registered with the endpoint **before** it is
04604	        started.  An endpoint that owns a worker only after the start
04605	        returned has no owner at all for a worker whose start did not
04606	        return, and the close of the endpoint could close the file
04607	        under a worker that is about to read from it.
04608
